<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
Let $(X,\ensuremath{\mathfrak M}, \mu)$ be a measure space with a $\sigma$-algebra $\ensuremath{\mathfrak M}$ and a measure $\mu$. A family of measurable sets $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is said to be a ball-basis if it satisfies the following conditions: 
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item[B1)] $0<\mu(B)<\infty$ for any ball $B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$.
		\item[B2)] For any points $x,y\in X$ there exists a ball $B\ni x,y$.
		\item[B3)] If $E\in \ensuremath{\mathfrak M}$, then for any $\varepsilon>0$ there exists a (finite or infinite) sequence of balls $B_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots$, such that $\mu(E\bigtriangleup \cup_k B_k)<\varepsilon$.
		\item[B4)] For any $B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ there is a ball $ [B]\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B} $ (called {\rm hull-ball} of $B$), satisfying the conditions
		\begin{align}
			&B^*=\bigcup_{A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}:\, \mu(A)\le 2\mu(B),\, A\cap B\neq\varnothing}A\subset  [B],\label{h12}\\
			&\qquad\qquad\mu\left([B]\right)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(B),\label{h13}
		\end{align}
		where $\ensuremath{\mathcal K}$ is a positive constant independent of $B$. 
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
We say that a ball basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is doubling if there is a constant $\eta>1$ such that for any ball $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$, with $B^*\neq X$, one can find a $B'\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$, satisfying
	\begin{equation}\label{h73}
		B\subsetneq B',\quad \mu(B')\le\eta  \cdot \mu(B).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
A ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is said to be separable if the union of an arbitrary collection of balls (not necessarily countable) is measurable.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
We say that sublinear operator $T:L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})\to L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb V})$ is a bounded oscillation operator if it satisfies the conditions
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item [T0)] (Weak type inequality) If $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$, then
		\begin{equation}\label{y57}
			\mu\{x\in B:\, \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_B)(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>\lambda \cdot \langle f\rangle_{B}\}\le\left(\frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0}{\lambda}\right)^{1/\rho}\mu(B),\quad \lambda>0,
		\end{equation}
		\item [T1)](Localization) for every $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ we have
		\begin{equation}\label{d7}
			{\rm OSC}_B\big(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})\big) \le\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\cdot \langle f \rangle^*_{B},
		\end{equation}
		\item [T2)](Connectivity) for any ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ ($A^*\neq X$) there exists a ball $B\supsetneq A$ (i.e. $B\supset A$, $B\neq A$) such that
		\begin{equation}\label{d20}
			{\rm SUP}_A\big(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*\setminus A^*})\big)\le\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2\cdot \langle f\rangle_{B^*},
		\end{equation}
	\end{enumerate}
whenever $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$. Here $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)$, $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)$ and $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)$ are constants depending only on the operator $T$.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
A collection of measurable sets (or balls) $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ is said to be sparse or $\gamma$-sparse if for any $B\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ there is a set $E_B\subset B$ such that $\mu(E_B)\ge \gamma\mu(B)$ and the sets $\{E_B:\, B\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}\}$ are pairwise disjoint, where  $0<\gamma<1$ is a constant.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
A family of sets $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ is said to be martingale type if every couple of elements $A,B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfies one of these three relations $A\cap B=\varnothing$, $A\subset B$ or $B\subset A$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
For two collection of sets $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ and $\bar \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ we write $\bar \ensuremath{\mathcal S} \Subset\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ if there is a one to one mapping from $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ to $\bar \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ taking every $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ to a $\bar A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ such that $\bar A\subset A$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{T7}
	Let $(X,\mu)$ be a measure space equipped with a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and $T$ be a ${\rm BO}$-operator from $L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ to $L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb V})$. Then for any function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ with ${\rm supp\,} f\subset B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ there exist a ball $B'\supset B$, as well as a $\gamma$-sparse family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and a martingale-family of measurable sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that
	\begin{align}
		& \bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}G\subset B',\quad \mu(B')\lesssim \mu(B),\label{y52}\\
		&\mu\big\{x\in X:\,\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x)>\lambda\big\}\lesssim \exp(-c\lambda)\mu(B),\quad \lambda>0,\label{y53}\\
		&\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\lesssim C(T)\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\left\langle f\right\rangle_{G}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x),\text { a.e. } x\in B,\label{y51}
	\end{align}
	where $0<\gamma<1$, $c>0$ are admissible constants and $C(T)=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)$. The same inequality holds also for the truncated operator $T^*$ from \eqref{y50}.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
We could omit conditions \eqref{y52} and \eqref{y53} in the statement of the theorem and write $\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_G$ instead of $\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}$ in \eqref{y51} likewise the sparse domination of \cite{Kar3}. With additional conditions  \eqref{y52} and \eqref{y53} we may capture some exponential decay inequalities proved first for classical Calderón–Zygmund operators in \cite{Kar2} a long time before the sparse domination results appeared, as well as for the ${\rm BO}$ operators proved in a recent work \cite{Kar1}.  Note that the exponential estimate of \cite{Kar1} is for ${\rm BO}$ operators with respect to doubling ball-bases. Applying Theorem \ref{T7} we extend this result on abstract measure spaces with general ball-bases.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
We say a BO operator is of restricted type if it satisfies R1)-R5) (in particular the ball-basis is doubling). A family of restricted type operators $\{T_\alpha\}$ is said to be uniformly restricted if conditions R3)-R5) are uniformly satisfied.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{R4}
	Notice that \eqref{y83} is a stronger version of T1)-condition, because of the additional logarithmic factor on the right hand side of \eqref{y83}.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{T8}
Let a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be doubling, $\{T_\alpha\}$ be a family of uniformly restricted ${\rm BO}$ operators and let $Tf=\sup_\alpha\|T_\alpha f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}$. If
\begin{equation}\label{y84}
	C(T)=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\sup_\alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)<\infty,
\end{equation}
then for any $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B$  there exist a ball $B'\supset B$, a sparse-collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and a martingale-collection of measurable sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfying \eqref{y52}, \eqref{y53} such that
	\begin{equation}\label{y94}
	|Tf(x)-m(B)|\lesssim C(T)\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\langle f\rangle_{\#,G}^*\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x), \quad x\in B,
\end{equation}		
where $m(B)=m_{T(f)}(B)$ is a median of the real valued function $T(f)$ on $B$ (see Definition \ref{D2}).
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{T5}
	Let $1\le r<\infty$ and $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be a doubling ball-basis. If a family of ${\rm BO}$ operators $\{T_\alpha\}$ satisfies R2)-R5) and the corresponding maximal operator $Tf=\sup_\alpha\|T_\alpha (f)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}$ satisfies \eqref{y84}, then the bound
	\begin{equation}\label{y30}
		{\rm OSC}_{B,\beta}\,T( f)\lesssim \left(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)(1-\beta)^{-1/r}+\sup_\alpha \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\right) \langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}.
	\end{equation}
	holds for any $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$, a ball $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and a number $0<\beta<1$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
If $\mu(X)<\infty$ and $T(\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_X)$ is a constant function, then $T$ trivially satisfies condition R5) with $B'=X$. We just note that $\mu(X)<\infty$ implies $X\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ (see Lemma \ref{L0}). Thus one can state simplified versions of Theorems \ref{T8} and \ref{T5} if $\mu (X)<\infty$.  Namely, we can suppose  $\{T_\alpha\}$ satisfies the restrictions R2)-R4) uniformly, as well as $T_\alpha(\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_X)\equiv c_\alpha$, $\|c_\alpha\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)$ instead of R5).
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
The statement of Theorem \ref{T5} and so Theorem \ref{T8} is true also when $T$ is simply a restricted type ${\rm BO}$ operator. One just need replace the right hand side of \eqref{y94} by 	$\|Tf(x)-m(B)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
Let $\omega:(0,1)\to(0,1)$ be a modulus of continuity, that is a monotone increasing and subadditive function. A family of non-negative real valued functions $\phi=\{\phi_B:\, B
	\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}\}\subset L^1(X)$ is said to be $\omega$-regular if 
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $\|\phi_B\|_{L^1(X)}=1$,
		\item there is an increasing function $\gamma:[1,\infty)\to [1,\infty)$ such that
		\begin{equation}
			\phi_B(x)\le \gamma\left(\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\phi_A(x)
		\end{equation}
		for every two balls $B\subset A$.
		\item there are positive constants $c_1,c_2$ such that for any ball $B$ we have the inequalities
		\begin{equation}\label{y4}
			c_1\cdot \frac{\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_B(x)}{\mu(B)}\le\phi_B(x)\le c_2\cdot \omega\left(\frac{\mu(B)}{d(x,B)}\right)\frac{1}{d(x,B)}, \quad x\in X .
		\end{equation}
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
A family of balls ${\mathscr G\,}(x)\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is said to be complete at a point $x\in X$ if 
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $x\in B$ for any $B\in {\mathscr G\,}(x)$, 
		\item  for any ball $A\ni x$ (not necessarily from ${\mathscr G\,}(x)$) there is a ball $\bar A\in {\mathscr G\,}(x)$ with $\bar A\supset A,\quad \mu(\bar A)\le \eta \mu(A)$,
	\end{enumerate}
	where $\eta \ge 1$ is a constant.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{T2}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be a doubling ball-basis. If a modulus of continuity satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{y7}
		\|\omega \|=1+\int_0^1\frac{\omega(t)\log(1/t)}{t}dt<\infty,
	\end{equation}
	then for any function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B$
	\begin{equation}\label{2}
		{\rm OSC}_{B,\alpha}(\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} (f))\lesssim \|\omega\|(1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B},\quad B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B},
	\end{equation}
	for any $0<\alpha<1$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{KC1}
	If a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is doubling, 	then for any $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B$  there exist a ball $B'\supset B$, a sparse-collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and a martingale-collection of measurable sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfying \eqref{y52}, \eqref{y53} such that
	\begin{equation}
	|\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)-m(B)|\lesssim \|\omega\|\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\langle f\rangle_{\#,G}^*\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x), \quad x\in B,
	\end{equation}		
	where $m(B)$ is a median of the real function $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} (f)$ on $B$ (see Definition \ref{D2}).
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{KC2}
	Under the assumptions of Theorem \ref{T2} the maximal function $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}}$ is bounded from ${\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ to ${\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb R})$. More precisely, 
	\begin{equation}
		\|\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}}(f)\|_{{\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb R})}\lesssim \|f\|_{{\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})}
	\end{equation}
	for any function
	\begin{equation}
		f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})\cap {\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U}).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
In the case when $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}}$ is the centered maximal operator on $\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d$, its boundedness on ${\rm BMO }(\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d)$ is the classical result of Bennett-DeVore-Sharpley \cite{BDS}. To the best of our knowledge this was the only known maximal function that is bounded on ${\rm BMO }$. The suggested proof of \cite{BDS} quite different and in particular it can not be applied for the non-centered maximal function on $\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d$.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{L0}
		If $(X,\mu)$ is a measure space equipped with a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and $\mu(X)<\infty$, then $X\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Fix an arbitrary point $x_0\in X$ and let $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be the family of balls containing $x_0$. By B2) condition we can write $X=\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A$. Then there exists a $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that $\mu(B)>\frac{1}{2} \sup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\mu(A)$ and using B4) condition we obtain
\begin{equation*}
X=\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A\subset B^*\subset [B].
\end{equation*}
Thus $X=[B]\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{L5}
	Let $(X,\mu)$ be a measure space equipped with a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$. Then there exists a sequence of balls $G_1\subset G_2\subset \ldots\subset G_n\subset \ldots$ such that $X=\cup_kG_k$. Moreover, for any ball $B$ there is a ball $G_n\supset B$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Fix an arbitrary $x_0\in X$ and let $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be the family of balls containing the point $x_0$. Choose a sequence $\eta_n\nearrow\eta=\sup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\mu(A)$, where $\eta$ can also be infinity. Let us see by induction that there is an increasing  sequence of balls $G_n\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that $\mu(G_n)> \eta_n$ and $G_n^*\subset G_{n+1}$. The base of induction is trivial. Suppose we have already chosen the first balls $G_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots, l$. There is a ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ so that $\mu(A)>\eta_{l+1}$. Let $A_l$ be the biggest in measure among two balls $A$ and $G_l$. By property B4) we have $A\cup G_l\subset A_l^*$ and there exists a ball $G_{l+1}\supset A_l^{**}$. This implies $\mu(G_{l+1})\ge \mu(A)>\eta_{l+1}$ and $G_{l+1}\supset G_l^*$, completing the induction.
	Let us see that $G_n$ is our desired sequence of balls. Indeed, let $B$ be an arbitrary ball. By B2) property there is a ball $A$ containing $x_0$, such that $A\cap B\neq \varnothing$. Then by property B4) we may find a ball $C\supset A\cup B$. For some $n$ we will have $\mu(C)\le 2\mu(G_n)$ and so once again using B4), we get $B\subset  C\subset G_{n}^*\subset G_{n+1}$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{L1-1}
	Let $(X,\mu)$ be a measure space with a ball bases $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$. If $E\subset X$ is bounded and a family of balls ${\mathscr G\,} $ is a covering of $E$, i.e. $E\subset \bigcup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}}G$, then there exists a finite or infinite sequence of pairwise disjoint balls $G_k\in {\mathscr G\,}$ such that $E \subset \bigcup_k G_k^{*}$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
The boundedness of $E$ implies $E\subset B$ for some $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$. If there is a ball $G\in{\mathscr G\,}$ so that $G\cap B\neq \varnothing$, $\mu(G)> \mu(B)$, then by two balls relation we will have $E\subset B\subset G^{*}$. Thus our desired sequence can be formed by a single element $G$. Hence we can suppose that every $G\in{\mathscr G\,}$ satisfies $G\cap B\neq \varnothing$, $\mu(G)\le \mu(B)$ and again by two balls relation $G\subset B^*$. Therefore, $\bigcup_{G\in{\mathscr G\,}}G\subset B^{*}$.
	Choose $G_1\in {\mathscr G\,}$, satisfying $\mu(G_1)> \frac{1}{2}\sup_{G\in{\mathscr G\,}}\mu(G)$. Then, suppose by induction we have already chosen elements $G_1,\ldots,G_k$ from ${\mathscr G\,}$. Choose $G_{k+1}\in {\mathscr G\,}$  disjoint with the balls $G_1,\ldots,G_k$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{b1}
		\mu(G_{k+1})> \frac{1}{2}\sup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}:\,G\cap G_j=\varnothing,\,j=1,\ldots,k}\mu(G).
	\end{equation}
	If for some $n$ we will not be able to determine $G_{n+1}$ the process will stop and we will get a finite sequence $G_1,G_2,\ldots, G_n$. Otherwise our sequence will be infinite. We shall consider the infinite case of the sequence (the finite case can be done similarly). Since the balls $G_n$ are pairwise disjoint and $G_n\subset B^{*}$, we have $\mu(G_n)\to 0$. Choose an arbitrary $G\in{\mathscr G\,}$ with $G\neq G_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots $ and let $m$ be the smallest integer satisfying
	$\mu(G)\ge 2\mu(G_{m+1})$.
	So we have $G \cap G_j\neq\varnothing$
	for some $1\le j\le m$, since otherwise by \eqref{b1}, $G$ had to be chosen instead of $G_{m+1}$. Besides, we have $\mu(G)< 2\mu(G_{j})$ because of the minimality property of $m$, and so by two balls relation $G\subset G_{j}^{*}$. Since $G\in {\mathscr G\,}$ was chosen arbitrarily, we get $E \subset\bigcup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}} G\subset \bigcup_k G_k^{*}$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
\label{D1}
	For a measurable set $E\subset X$ a point $x\in E$ is said to be density point if for any $\varepsilon>0$ there exists a ball $B\ni x$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu(B\cap E)>(1-\varepsilon )\mu(B).
	\end{equation*} 
	We say a ball basis satisfies the density property if for any measurable set $E$ almost all points $x\in E$ are density points.
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{L12}
	Every ball basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ satisfies the density condition.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Applying Lemma \ref{L5} one can check that it is enough to establish the density property for the bounded measurable sets. Suppose to the contrary there exist a bounded measurable set $E\subset X$ together its subset $F\subset E$ (maybe non-measurable) with an outer measure
\begin{equation}
	\mu^*(F)=\inf_{A\supset F,\,A\in \ensuremath{\mathfrak M}}\mu(A)>0
\end{equation}
 such that
	\begin{equation}\label{z38}
		\mu(B\setminus E)>\alpha\mu(B) \text{ whenever }B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B},\, B\cap F\neq\varnothing,
	\end{equation}
where $0<\alpha<1$. According to the definition of the outer measure one can find a measurable set $\bar F$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{z2}
		F\subset \bar F\subset E, \quad \mu(\bar F)<\mu^*(F) +\varepsilon.
	\end{equation}
	By B3)-condition there is a sequence of balls $B_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots $, such that
	\begin{equation}\label{z42}	
		\mu\left(\bar F\bigtriangleup (\cup_k B_k)\right)<\varepsilon.
	\end{equation}
	We discuss two kind of balls $B_k$, satisfying either $B_k\cap F=\varnothing$ or $B_k\cap F\neq\varnothing$. For the first collection we have 
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu^*(F)\le \mu\left(\bar F\setminus \bigcup_{k:\,B_k\cap F=\varnothing} B_k\right)\le \mu(\bar F)<\mu^*(F)+\varepsilon,
	\end{equation*}
	which implies 
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu\left(\bar F\bigcap \left(\bigcup_{k:\,B_k\cap F=\varnothing} B_k\right)\right)=\mu(\bar F)- \mu\left(\bar F\setminus \bigcup_{k:\,B_k\cap F=\varnothing} B_k\right)<\varepsilon.
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, combining also \eqref{z42} we obtain
	\begin{equation}\label{z3}
		\mu\left(\bigcup_{k:\,B_k\cap F=\varnothing} B_k\right)<2\varepsilon.
	\end{equation}
For the balls $B_k\cap F\neq\varnothing$, using \eqref{z38} and \eqref{z2} we have
	\begin{equation}\label{z41}
		\mu(B_k\setminus \bar F)\ge \mu(B_k\setminus E)>\alpha \mu(B_k),\quad k=1,2,\ldots.
	\end{equation}
		Since $E$ and so $\bar F$ are bounded, applying Lemma \ref{L1-1} and \eqref{z42}, one can find a subsequence of pairwise disjoint balls $\tilde B_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots$, such that
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu\left(\bar F\setminus\cup_k\tilde B_k^{*}\right)<\varepsilon.
	\end{equation*} 
	Thus, from B4)-condition, \eqref{z42}, \eqref{z3} and \eqref{z41}, we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		\mu^*(F)< \mu(\bar F)&\le \mu\left(\cup_k\tilde B_k^{*}\right)+\varepsilon\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\sum_k\mu(\tilde B_k)+\varepsilon\\
		&= \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu\left(\bigcup_{k:\,\tilde B_k\cap F=\varnothing} \tilde B_k\right)+\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\sum_{k:\,\tilde B_k\cap F\neq\varnothing} \mu(\tilde B_k)+\varepsilon\\
		&<2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\varepsilon+\frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\alpha}\sum_k\mu(\tilde B_k\setminus \bar F)+\varepsilon\\
		&\le (2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}+1)\varepsilon+\frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\alpha}\mu\left(\bar F\bigtriangleup( \cup_k B_k)\right)<\varepsilon\left(2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}+1+\frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\alpha}\right).
	\end{align*}
	Since $\varepsilon $ can be arbitrarily small, we get $\mu(F)=0$ and so a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{L1-2}  Let $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and ${\mathscr G\,}$ be a family of pairwise disjoint balls such that each $G\in {\mathscr G\,}$ satisfies the relations 
	\begin{align}
		&G^*\cap A \neq \varnothing,\label{y36}\\
		& 0<c_1\le \mu(G)\le c_2.\label{a41}
	\end{align}
	Then ${\mathscr G\,}$ is finite and for the number of its elements we have $\#{\mathscr G\,}\lesssim\max\{c_2, \mu(A)\}/c_1$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Suppose $G_1,G_2,\ldots, G_N$ are some elements of ${\mathscr G\,}$ with hull-balls satisfying $\mu([G_1])\ge \max_j\mu([G_i])$. Consider the ball
	\begin{align}\label {a42}
		B=\left\{
		\begin{array}{lc}
			A&\hbox{ if } \mu(A)>\mu([G_1]),\\
			\left[G_1\right]&\hbox { if } \mu(A)\le \mu([G_1]).\\
		\end{array}
		\right.
	\end{align}
	Using \eqref{y36}, one can check that for both cases in \eqref{a42} we have $G_k\subset [G_k]\subset B^{**}$, $k=1,\ldots, N$.	Thus, since $G_k$ are pairwise disjoint, from \eqref{a41} we obtain
	\begin{align}
		N\cdot c_1&\le \mu\left(\cup_{k} G_k\right)\le \mu(B^{**})\\
		&\le\max\{\mu(A^{**}),\mu([G_1]^{**})\}\lesssim \max\{c_2, \mu(A)\},
	\end{align}
	which completes the proof of lemma.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{R2}
	One can prove a similar lemma with $G^{**}\cap A \neq \varnothing$ instead of \eqref{y36}.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{L2}
	If $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is a doubling ball-basis, then for any ball $B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ with $B^*\neq X$ there is a ball $B'$, satisfying 
	\begin{equation}\label{x42}
		B\subsetneq B',\quad 2\mu(B)\le \mu( B')\le\eta\ensuremath{\mathcal K} \mu(B).	
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Let $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$, $B^{*}\neq X$. Suppose to the contrary there is no ball $B'$ satisfying \eqref{x42} and denote
	\begin{equation*}
		\ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}:\,B\subseteq A,\, \mu(A)\le \eta\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(B)\}.
	\end{equation*}
	According to the assumption every $A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ should satisfy the bound $\mu(A)<2\mu(B)$. This and B4) imply
$\bigcup_{A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A\subset B^*\subset [B]\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$, where $\mu([B])\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(B)$. Thus, we have $[B]\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and so 
$\bigcup_{A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A= B^*=[B]$. On the other hand, according to the doubling condition, there is a ball 
$A'\supsetneq [B]$
	such that $\mu(A')\le\eta\cdot \mu([B])\le \eta\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(B)$. The latter implies $A'\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and therefore $A'\subset [B]$ that gives a contradiction. Lemma is proved.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{L1}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be a doubling ball basis. Then for any balls $A\subset B$ there exists a sequence of balls $A=A_0\subset A_1\subset \ldots\subset A_n=[B]$ such that $\mu(A_{k+1})\lesssim \mu(A_k)$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
The case of $\mu(B)\le 2\mu([A])$ is trivial, we may simply choose $A_0=A$ and $A_1=B$. So we can suppose $\mu(B)> 2\mu([A])$. Applying Lemma \ref{L2} we may find a sequence of balls $A=B_0, B_1,\ldots, B_{n-1}$ such that $[B_k]\subset B_{k+1}$ and $2\mu([B_k])\le \mu(B_{k+1})\le \eta \ensuremath{\mathcal K} \mu([B_k])$, $\mu(B)\le\mu(B_{n-1})\lesssim \mu(B)$. Obviously, we may define our desired sequence by $A_0=A$, $A_n=[B]$ and $A_k=[B_k]$, $k=1,2,\ldots,n-1$.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{L8}
	Let $F$ and $E\subset F$ be measurable sets, where $E$ is bounded as well. Then there exists a countable family of balls ${\mathscr G\,}={\mathscr G\,}(E)$ such that
	\begin{align}
		&E\subset_{a.e.} \bigcup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}}G, \label{a14}\\
		&\sum_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}}\mu(G)\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(F).\label{a27}
	\end{align}
Besides, for any ball $G'\supsetneq G \in {\mathscr G\,}$ we have
	\begin{equation}\label{aa15}
		\mu(G'\cap F)<\mu(G')/2.
	\end{equation}
	If $G$ is doubling, then \eqref{aa15} (with the constant $2\eta\ensuremath{\mathcal K}$ on the right-hand side) holds also for $G'=G$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
First consider a general ball-basis. Let $D$ be the density points set of $F$ (see Definition \ref{D1}). Thus for any $x\in D$ the family of balls
	\begin{equation*}
		{\mathscr {C}}_x=\{C\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}:\, x\in C,\,\mu(C\cap F)\ge\mu(C)/2\}
	\end{equation*}
	is nonempty and therefore there is  a ball $B_x\in {\mathscr {C}}_x$ such that  $\mu(B_x)>\frac{1}{2}\sup_{C\in {\mathscr {C}}_x}\mu(C)$.
	From the two ball relation it follows that $\cup_{C\in {\mathscr {C}}_x}C\subset B_x^*$.
	Thus, if a ball $G'$ satisfies $G'\supsetneq B_x^*$, then $x\in G'\notin {\mathscr {C}}$ and so
	\begin{equation}\label{y8}
		\mu(G'\cap F)<\mu(G')/2.
	\end{equation}
	Since $\{B_x:\,x\in E\cap D\}$ is a ball-covering for $E\cap D$, applying Lemma \ref{L1-1}, we find sequence of pairwise disjoint balls $\{B_{x_k}\}$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{y5}
		E\cap D\subset \cup_kB_{x_k}^*.
	\end{equation}
	Choose a hull-ball $G_k\supset B_{x_k}^*$ such that $\mu(G_k)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(B_{x_k})$. We claim that the family ${\mathscr G\,}(E)=\{G_k\}$ satisfies the conditions of lemma. Indeed, \eqref{aa15} follows from the observation in \eqref{y8}.  By density Lemma \ref{L12} we have $\mu(F\setminus D)=0$ and combining also \eqref{y5}, we obtain \eqref{a14}. Then, since 
	$B_x\in {\mathscr {C}}_x$, it follows that
	\begin{align}
		\sum_k\mu(G_k)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K} \sum_k\mu(B_{x_k})< 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K} \sum_k\mu(B_{x_k}\cap F)\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K} \mu(F)
	\end{align}
	that gives \eqref{a27}. This completes the proof in the case of non-doubling ball-basis. Now let $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is doubling. Applying the above procedure first we get ${\mathscr G\,}(E)$, then replace each $G\in {\mathscr G\,}(E)$ by a ball $\tilde G\supsetneq G$ satisfying $\mu(\tilde G)\le \eta\mu(G)$. Clearly, all the conditions of Lemma \ref{L8} will be satisfied for the new collection as well, besides we will have \eqref{aa15} for $G'=G$.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{R3}
	Obviously we can also claim in the statement of Lemma \ref{L8} an extra condition 
	\begin{equation}\label{y9}
		E\cap G\neq\varnothing,\quad G\in {\mathscr G\,}(E).
	\end{equation}
	One just need to remove those balls from ${\mathscr G\,}(E)$, which do not satisfy \eqref{y9}.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
A family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ is said to be a tree-collection with a root-ball $A_0\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ if for each $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ there is an attached children-collection ${\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ (it can also be empty), $A\notin {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$, such that for every element $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ there is a sequence of balls $\{A_0, A_1, \ldots, A_n=A\}\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ with $A_{j+1}\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A_j)$, $j=0,1,\ldots,n-1$. The relation $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$ otherwise will be denoted by $A=\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G)$, meaning that $A$ is the parent of $G$.  We say $G$ is in the generation of a ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ if $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}^n(A)$ for some $n\ge 1$. The generation of a ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will be denoted by ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)=\cup_{n\ge 1}{\mathfrak {Ch}}^n(A)$. By the definition for the root-ball we have ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(A_0)=\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\setminus \{A_0\}$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{P11}
	Let $\{F_B:\, B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}\}$ be a family of measurable sets such that $\mu(F_B)<\alpha \mu(B)$, where $0<\alpha<1/10\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^7$. Then for any ball $A_0$ one can find a countable sparse-tree-collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ containing a double-hull ball $[[A_0]]$ as a root-ball, such that 
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item [a1)] $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\,\Rightarrow \, G\subset  A$,
		\item [a2)] $A_0\subset_{a.e.} \cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}A\setminus F_{A}$
		\item [a3)] for any $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ it holds the bound
		\begin{equation}\label{s27}
			\sum_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)}\mu(G)\lesssim\alpha  \mu(A),
		\end{equation}
		\item [a4)] if $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$, then for any ball and $G'\supsetneq G$
		\begin{equation}\label{y27}
			\mu(G'\cap F_A)< \mu(G')/2\text { if }G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A),
		\end{equation}
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
First we construct a tree-collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ with the root-ball $A_0$, then the desired sparse family $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ will be obtained applying certain removal procedures on $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. The elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will be determined inductively by an increasing order of generations levels. 
	First, we apply Lemma \ref{L8} for $F=F_{[[A_0]]}$ and $E=[A_0]\cap F_{[[A_0]]}$. We get a child-ball collection ${\mathfrak {Ch}}(A_0)={\mathscr G\,}(E)$ satisfying conditions \eqref{a14}-\eqref{aa15}. Then we do the same with every $A\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A_0)$, again applying Lemma \ref{L8} for $F=F_{[[A]]}$ and $E=[A]\cap F_{[[A]]}$. With this we obtain the second generation ${\mathfrak {Ch}}^2(A_0)$. Continuing this procedure to infinity we will get generation families  ${\mathfrak {Ch}}^n(A_0)$, $n=0,1,2,\ldots$, which union we denote by $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. By the claims of Lemma \ref{L8} (see also Remark \ref{R3}) for every $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ we have
	\begin{align}
		&[A]\cap F_{[[A]]}\cap G\neq\varnothing,\quad G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A), \label{s9}\\
		&[A]\cap F_{[[A]]}\subset_{a.e.} \bigcup_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)}G, \quad \label{s14}\\
		&\sum_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)}\mu(G)\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu(F_{[[A]]})\\
		&\qquad\qquad\quad\le 2\alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal K}  \mu([[A]])\le 2 \alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^3\mu(A)< \mu(A)/5\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^4,\label{y72}\\
		&\mu(G'\cap F_{[[A]]})<\mu(G')/2,\label{s15}
	\end{align}
	where \eqref{s15} holds for any ball $G'\supset [G],\, G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. Besides, from \eqref{y72} we get $\mu(G^{**})\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^2\mu(G)<\mu(A)$ and so $G^{**}\subset A^{**}$, provided $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. The latter implies 
	\begin{equation}\label{y11}
		G^{**}\subset A^{**}\text{ if }G\in {\mathfrak {Gen}} (A).
	\end{equation}
	
	Now we apply removal procedures on $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, removing some elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. As we will see below, 
	removing an element $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, we also remove all the elements of its generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}} (A)$. Thus relations \eqref{s9}, \eqref{y72} and \eqref{s15} keep holding during the entire process of reduction. 
	
	To start the description of the process, we let $R=\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^2$ and for $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ denote ${\rm r}(B)=[\log_R \mu(B)]$. Since $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$ implies $\mu(G)< \mu(A)/R$ (see \eqref{y72} ), we can say
	\begin{equation}\label{y73}
		G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow {\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A).
	\end{equation}
	Thus the collections of balls
	\begin{align*}
		\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_k&=\{B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(B)=k\}\\
		&=\left\{B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, R^{k}\le \mu(B)<R^{k+1}\right\},\quad k\le k_0={\rm r}(A_0),
	\end{align*}
	give a partition of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, i.e. we have $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\cup_{k\le k_0} \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_k$, where $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0}=\{A_0\}$. 
	The removal of the elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will be realized in different stages. The content of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0}$ will not be changed. In the $n$-th stage only the contents of the families $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_k$ with $k\le k_0-n$ can be changed. Besides, at the end of the $n$-th stage $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-n}$ will be fixed and remain the same till the end of the process. Suppose by induction the $l$-th stage of the process has been already finished and so the families $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_k$, $k=k_0,k_0-1,k_0-2,\ldots, k_0-l$ have already fixed. In the next $(l+1)$-th stage we will apply the following two procedures consecutively:
	\begin{procedure} Remove any element $G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ together with all the elements of his generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(G)$ if there exists a $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfying the conditions  
		\begin{align}	
			&G^{**}\cap B\neq \varnothing ,\label{d29}\\
			&{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G))\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll {\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^{k+1}(G)),\label{d32}
		\end{align}
		for some integer $k\ge 0$ (for $\ll$ see the notation before Proposition \ref{P11}).
	\end{procedure}
	\begin{remark}
		Observe that if an element $G$ is removed because of a ball $B$ satisfying conditions \eqref{d29} and \eqref{d32} of Procedure 1, then we should have 
		\begin{equation}\label{y10}
			{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G))
		\end{equation}
		that means at any time of the process \eqref{d32} can hold only with $k=0$. Indeed, the left hand side inequality in \eqref{y10} immediately follows from \eqref{d32}. To prove the right one, suppose to the contrary \eqref{d32} holds with $k\ge 1$. Thus, according to \eqref{y73}, we can write
		\begin{equation*}
			G'=\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G)\in \bigcup_{j=0}^{l}\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-j}. 
		\end{equation*}
		Since $G'^{**}\supset G^{**}$ (see \eqref{y11}), we have $G'^{**}\cap B\neq \varnothing $. On the other hand  \eqref{d32} can be written by ${\rm r}(G')\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G'))$. We thus conclude that $G'$ satisfies the conditions of the Procedure 1, so $G'$  together with his generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(G')$ (include $G$) had to be removed in one of the previous stages of the process. This is a contradiction and so $k=0$. 
		\begin{remark}
			We also observe that if some $G$ is removed because of \eqref{d29} and \eqref{d32}, then the ball $B$ in \eqref{d32} will never be removed from $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ during the entire process of induction.
		\end{remark}
	\end{remark}
	\begin{procedure}
		Apply Lemma \ref{L1-1} to the rest of the elements $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ having after Procedure 1. The application of Lemma \ref{L1-1} removes some more elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$. If an element $A$ is removed, then the generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)$ will also be removed. 
	\end{procedure}
	\begin{remark}
		After the Procedure 2 the elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ become pairwise disjoint. Besides, we will have   
		\begin{equation}\label{a70}
			\bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}(\text{\rm before Procedure 2})} G\subset \bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}(\text{\rm after Procedure  2})}G^{*}.
		\end{equation}
	\end{remark}
	After these two procedures the family $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ will be fixed. Hence, finishing the induction process, we get the final state of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ which will be denoted by $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. Since after Procedure 2 in the $n$-th stage $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-n}$ gets countable number of balls so the family $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will also be countable at the end of whole process. 
	
	Now we shall prove that for an admissible constant $\alpha>0$ the family $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ is a union of two $1/2$-sparse collections of balls. For $A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ define
	\begin{equation}\label{d25}
		E(A)=A\setminus \bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\,{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(A)} G
		=A\setminus \bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\,G\cap A\neq\varnothing,\,{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(A)}G.
	\end{equation}
	Observe that 
	\begin{equation}\label{y13}
		E(A)\cap E(B)=\varnothing,\text { if } {\rm r}(A)\not \asymp {\rm r}(B) \text { or } {\rm r}(A)= {\rm r}(B).
	\end{equation}
	Indeed, take arbitrary $A,B\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. If ${\rm r}(A)={\rm r}(B)$, then the balls $A,B$ as a result of the application of Procedure 2 (Lemma \ref{L1-1}) are pairwise disjoint. Therefore from \eqref{d25} it follows that $E(A)\cap E(B)=\varnothing$. If ${\rm r}(A)\gg{\rm r}(B)$, then $E(A)\cap B=\varnothing$
	immediately follows from definition \eqref{d25} and so we will again have $E(A)\cap E(B)=\varnothing$. 
	To prove 
	\begin{equation}\label{aa28}
		\mu(E(A))\ge \mu(A)/2
	\end{equation}
	take an arbitrary $A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and denote
	\begin{equation*}
		\ensuremath{\mathscr P}=\ensuremath{\mathscr P}_A=\{P\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(P)\asymp{\rm r}(A),\,P^{**}\cap A\neq\varnothing\}.
	\end{equation*} 
	We have 
	\begin{equation}\label{h55}
		R^{-2}\cdot \mu(A)\le \mu(P)\le R^2\cdot \mu(A),\quad P\in \ensuremath{\mathscr P},
	\end{equation}
as well as
	\begin{equation*}
		\ensuremath{\mathscr P}\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{l-1}\cup\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_l\cup\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{l+1},
	\end{equation*}
	where $l={\rm r}(A)$. Hence $\ensuremath{\mathscr P}$ consists of three families of pairwise disjoint balls (see Procedure 2). Thus, applying  Lemma \ref{L1-2} (see also Remark \ref{R2}), we get 
	\begin{equation}\label{a84}
		\#\ensuremath{\mathscr P}\le\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^4.
	\end{equation}
	Suppose that $G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfies
	\begin{equation*}
		{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(A),\quad G\cap A\neq\varnothing,  
	\end{equation*}
	and so $G^{**}\cap A\neq\varnothing$. Since $G$ has not been removed via Procedure 1, we have ${\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G))\asymp{\rm r}(A)$ for some integer $k\ge 1$. Denote $P=\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G)\in \bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. We have ${\rm r}(P)\asymp{\rm r}(A)$ as well as by \eqref{y11} $P^{**}\supset G^{**}$ and so $P^{**}\cap A\neq\varnothing$. This implies that $P\in\ensuremath{\mathscr P}$ and $G\in {\mathfrak {Gen}}(P)$.  Hence, from \eqref{y72}, \eqref{d25}, \eqref{h55} and  \eqref{a84} it follows that 
	\begin{align*}
		\mu(A\setminus E(A))&\le \mu\left(\bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\,G\cap A\neq\varnothing,\,{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(A)}G\right)\\
		&\le \mu\left(\bigcup_{P\in \ensuremath{\mathscr P}}\bigcup_{G\in {\mathfrak {Gen}}(P)}G\right)\le \sum_{P\in \ensuremath{\mathscr P}}\sum_{k=1}^\infty \mu\left(\bigcup_{G:\,\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G)=P}G\right)\\
		&\le \sum_{P\in \ensuremath{\mathscr P}}\sum_{k=1}^\infty (5\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^4)^{-k}\mu(P)< \mu(A)/2.  
	\end{align*}
	Thus we get \eqref{aa28}. From \eqref{y13} and \eqref{aa28} one can easily conclude that two families  
	\begin{align}
	&\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_1=\{A\in\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(A)\text { is odd}\},\\
		& \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_2=\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\setminus \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_1=	\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_1=\{A\in\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(A)\text { is even}\},
	\end{align}
	are $1/2$-sparse.  Let us see that for any $A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ the set
	\begin{equation}\label{d31}
		Q_A=\left([A]\cap F_{[[A]]}\right)\setminus\bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)}G^{*}
	\end{equation}
is a null set. According to \eqref{s14} it  is enough to prove that 
	\begin{equation}\label{d28}
		D=\bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)}G^{*}
		\supset \bigcup_{G\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, G\in{\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)}G.
	\end{equation}
	Take $A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and arbitrary $G\in{\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. We have ${\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)$. In the case $G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, that is $G$ has not  been removed from $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ during the Procedures 1 and 2, $G$ is an element of the left union of \eqref{d28} and so $G\subset D$. If $G\not\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, then $G$ has been removed during the removal process. If $G$ was removed by an application of Procedure 1, then there exists a ball $B\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that $G^{**}\cap B\neq \varnothing$ and ${\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G))={\rm r}(A)$ (see two remarks after Procedure 1). On the other hand for a double hull-ball of $[[G]]$ we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu([[G]])\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^2\mu(G)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^2\cdot \frac{\mu(B)}{R}= \mu(B).
	\end{equation*}
	Thus we get $G\subset G^{**}\subset [[G]]\subset B^{*}$, which means $G\subset D$. If $G$ was removed by an application of Procedure 2, then according to \eqref{a70} we have $G\subset \cup_kG_k^{*}$ for a family of balls $G_k$ satisfying ${\rm r}(G_k)={\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)$ and so $G_k^{*}\subset D$. This again implies $G\subset D$ and so we get \eqref{d31}. Now observe that 
	\begin{equation*}
		E=\bigcap_{k\le k_0} \bigcup_{G\in\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)\le k}G^{*}
	\end{equation*}
	is a null-set, since $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ consists of countable number of balls with bounded sum of their measures (see \eqref{y72}). Since the sets \eqref{d31} are null sets, so is $F=\cup_{A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}}Q_A$. Choose an arbitrary $x\in [A_0]\setminus (E\cup F)$. From $x\notin E$ it follows that
	\begin{equation}
		x\in \bigcup_{G\in\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)\le k+1}G^{*}\setminus \bigcup_{G\in\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)\le k}G^{*}
	\end{equation}
for an integer $k$. Thus there exists a ball $A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ (with ${\rm r}(A)=k+1$) such that
	\begin{equation}\label{y79}
		x\in A^{*}\setminus \bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\,{\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)}G^{*}\subset  [A]\setminus \bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\,{\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)}G^{*}.
	\end{equation}  
Since $x\notin F$, we can write
\begin{equation}\label{y80}
	x\notin Q_A=\left([A]\cap F_{[[A]]}\right)\setminus\bigcup_{G\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}:\, {\rm r}(G)<{\rm r}(A)}G^{*}.
\end{equation}
From \eqref{y79} and \eqref{y80} we conclude $x\in [A]\setminus F_{[[A]]}$. Hence we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{y74}
	A_0\subset [A_0]\subset_{a.e.}\bigcup_{A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}}[A]\setminus F_{[[A]]}\subset \bigcup_{A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}}[[A]]\setminus F_{[[A]]}.
\end{equation}
One can now check that $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}=\{[[A]]:\, A\in \bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\}$ is the desired sparse tree-collection, satisfying conditions a1)-a4), where the children relationship is the same as we had in $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. Indeed, \eqref{y74} $\Rightarrow$ a2), \eqref{s15} $\Rightarrow$ a4). An intermediate inequality in \eqref{y72} $\Rightarrow$ a3).
From  \eqref{y72} and T2) condition it easily follows that $\mu([[G]])\le \mu([A])$ whenever $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. Thus, using also \eqref{s9} we conclude $[[G]]\subset [[A]]$, that implies a1). Hence the proof is complete.

.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- procedure-1: body_md -->
Remove any element $G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ together with all the elements of his generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(G)$ if there exists a $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ satisfying the conditions  
		\begin{align}	
			&G^{**}\cap B\neq \varnothing ,\label{d29}\\
			&{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G))\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll {\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^{k+1}(G)),\label{d32}
		\end{align}
		for some integer $k\ge 0$ (for $\ll$ see the notation before Proposition \ref{P11}).
<!-- end procedure-1 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
Observe that if an element $G$ is removed because of a ball $B$ satisfying conditions \eqref{d29} and \eqref{d32} of Procedure 1, then we should have 
		\begin{equation}\label{y10}
			{\rm r}(G)\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G))
		\end{equation}
		that means at any time of the process \eqref{d32} can hold only with $k=0$. Indeed, the left hand side inequality in \eqref{y10} immediately follows from \eqref{d32}. To prove the right one, suppose to the contrary \eqref{d32} holds with $k\ge 1$. Thus, according to \eqref{y73}, we can write
		\begin{equation*}
			G'=\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}^k(G)\in \bigcup_{j=0}^{l}\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-j}. 
		\end{equation*}
		Since $G'^{**}\supset G^{**}$ (see \eqref{y11}), we have $G'^{**}\cap B\neq \varnothing $. On the other hand  \eqref{d32} can be written by ${\rm r}(G')\ll{\rm r}(B)\ll{\rm r}(\ensuremath{\mathrm {pr}}(G'))$. We thus conclude that $G'$ satisfies the conditions of the Procedure 1, so $G'$  together with his generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(G')$ (include $G$) had to be removed in one of the previous stages of the process. This is a contradiction and so $k=0$. 
		\begin{remark}
			We also observe that if some $G$ is removed because of \eqref{d29} and \eqref{d32}, then the ball $B$ in \eqref{d32} will never be removed from $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ during the entire process of induction.
		\end{remark}
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
We also observe that if some $G$ is removed because of \eqref{d29} and \eqref{d32}, then the ball $B$ in \eqref{d32} will never be removed from $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ during the entire process of induction.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- procedure-2: body_md -->
Apply Lemma \ref{L1-1} to the rest of the elements $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ having after Procedure 1. The application of Lemma \ref{L1-1} removes some more elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$. If an element $A$ is removed, then the generation ${\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)$ will also be removed.
<!-- end procedure-2 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
After the Procedure 2 the elements of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}$ become pairwise disjoint. Besides, we will have   
		\begin{equation}\label{a70}
			\bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}(\text{\rm before Procedure 2})} G\subset \bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}_{k_0-l-1}(\text{\rm after Procedure  2})}G^{*}.
		\end{equation}
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{L13}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ be a countable tree-collection of measurable sets in $X$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{y44}
		G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow G\subset A,
	\end{equation} 
	and let $\{E_A\subset A:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\}$ be another family of measurable sets. Then there exists a martingale family of sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{\bar A\subset A:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\}\Subset\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that the sets $\bar A\cap E_A$, $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, are pairwise disjoint and 
		\begin{align}
		&G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow\bar G\subset \bar A,\label{y67}\\
		&G\notin {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow\bar G\cap  \bar A=\varnothing,\label{y68}\\
		&\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}E_A=\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\bar A\cap E_A.\label{y64}
	\end{align}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Without loss of generality we can suppose that
	\begin{equation}\label{y70}
		\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A=\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}E_A,
	\end{equation}
since otherwise one can replace every $G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ by $G\cap \left(\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}E_A\right)$.
The family $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will be constructed  inductively as follows. Since $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ is countable we can write $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{A_k,\, k=1,2,\ldots\}$, using an arbitrary numeration as well as keeping the same children relation between the elements. We will inductively change the terms of $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ as follows. At the $n$-th stage of induction every element $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ will be replaced by $A\setminus A_n\cap E_{A_n}$ provided $A_n\notin \{A\}\cup {\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)$. Finishing the induction procedure we will finally get the desired sequence $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{\bar A_k,\, k=1,2,\ldots\}$ as a limiting version of the sequence $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}_n$. Let us check the claims of the lemma. 1) To prove that $\bar A_k\cap E_{A_k}$ are pairwise disjoint let $\bar A_n$ and $\bar A_j$ be two different elements of $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$. Without loss of generality we can suppose that $A_n\notin {\mathfrak {Gen}}(A_j)$. So one can check that  after the $n$-th stage of induction $A_n\cap E_{A_n}$ and $A_j\cap E_{A_j}$ became disjoint, staying disjoint during the next stages of induction. 2) For \eqref{y64} one just need to check that the union $\cup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}A\cap E_A$ is not changed after any stage of induction. 3) To prove relation \eqref{y67} observe that for two sets $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$ the relation $G\subset A$
that we have at the beginning, will be satisfied after any stage of induction.  Indeed, if we are at the $n$-th stage and $A_n\in \{A\}\cup {\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)$, then $A$ is not changed by the $n$-th stage action.  If $A_n\notin \{A\}\cup {\mathfrak {Gen}}(A)$, then both $G$ and $A$ are reduced similarly. So in both cases we will still have $G\subset A$. 4) To show \eqref{y68} we need to see that if $A$ and $B$ are not in a parental relationship, then $\bar A\cap \bar B=\varnothing $. By \eqref{y64} and \eqref{y70} it is enough to show that
	\begin{equation}\label{y66}
	\bar A\cap \bar B\cap (\bar A_n\cap E_{A_n})=\varnothing
	\end{equation}
	for any $n=1,2,\ldots$. It is clear that each $A_n$ is not in the generation of either $A$ or $B$. Thus the set $\bar A_n\cap E_{A_n}$ has been removed at least from one of the sets $A$ or $B$ at the $n$-th stage of the induction, which yields \eqref{y66}. With this we complete the proof of lemma.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{T1-1}
	The maximal operator \eqref{1-1} satisfies the bound
	\begin{equation}\label{h38}
		\mu\left\{x\in X:\, \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)>\lambda\right\}\le \frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\lambda^{1/\rho}}\left(\int_{X }\| f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho/\rho},\quad \lambda>0,
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Consider the set $E=\{ x\in X:\, \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)>\lambda\}$, which can be non-measurable. For any $x\in  E $ there exists a ball $B(x)\subset X$ such that
	\begin{equation*}
		x\in B(x),\quad \frac{1}{\mu(B(x))^{\rho}}\left(\int_{B(x)}\|f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho}>\lambda,
	\end{equation*}
which implies
\begin{equation}
	\mu(B(x))<\frac{1}{\lambda^{1/\rho}}\left(\int_{B(x)}\|f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho/\rho}.
\end{equation}
	We have $E=\cup_{x\in E} B(x)$. Given $G\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ consider the collection of balls $\{B(x):\, x\in E\cap G\}$. Applying Lemma \ref{L1-1}, we find a sequence of pairwise disjoint balls $\{B_k\}$ taken from this collection such that $E \cap G\subset \cup_kB_k^*=Q(G)$.
	Applying the inequality $\sum_kx_k^d\le (\sum_kx_k)^d$ for positive numbers $x_k$ and for $d=\varrho/\rho\ge 1$, we get
	\begin{align}
		\mu(E\cap G)&\le \mu(Q(G))	\le \sum_k \mu(B_k^*)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K} \sum_k \mu(B_k)\\
		&\le \frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\lambda^{1/\rho}}\sum_k \left(\int_{B_k}\|f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r \right)^{ \varrho/\rho}\le \frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\lambda^{1/\rho}}\left(\int_{X }\|f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho/\rho}.
	\end{align}
	According to Lemma \ref{L5} there is a sequence of balls $G_1\subset G_2\subset \ldots $ such that $X=\cup_{k}G_k$,
	so we conclude
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu(E)=\lim_{n\to\infty }\mu(E\cap G_n)\le \frac{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}{\lambda^{1/\rho}}\left(\int_{X }\|f\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho/\rho}
	\end{equation*}
	and so \eqref{h38}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{L14}
	If a sublinear operator $T$ satisfies T1), then for any $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$  and a $\gamma$-ball $B$ we have 
		\begin{equation}\label{y60}
			{\rm OSC}_B\big(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})\big) \lesssim\gamma^\rho \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\langle f \rangle^*_{B},
		\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $B$ be a $\gamma$-ball so there are balls $B_1\subset B\subset B_2$ such that $\mu(B_2)\le \gamma \mu(B_1)$.
	Thus for $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ we can write
	\begin{align}
		{\rm OSC}_B\big(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})\big)&={\rm OSC}_B\big(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B_1^*})\big)\\
		&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\cdot \langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle^*_{B_1}\le2 \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\cdot \langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle_{\bar B_1}
	\end{align}
	for some ball $\bar B_1\supset B_1$. If $\mu(\bar B_1)\le \mu(B_2)$, then we have $\bar B_1\subset B_2^*$ and therefore
	\begin{equation*}
		\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle_{\bar B_1}\le  \left(\frac{\mu(B_2^*)}{\mu( \bar B_1)}\right)^\rho\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle_{B_2^*}\le (\gamma\ensuremath{\mathcal K})^\rho\langle f \rangle^*_{B}.
	\end{equation*}
	Otherwise, if $\mu(\bar B_1)\ge \mu(B_2)$, then we will have 
	$B\subset B_2\subset \bar B_1^{*}$ and then
	\begin{equation}
		\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle_{\bar B_1}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^\rho\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}\rangle_{\bar B_1^*}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^\rho\langle f\rangle_{B}^*.
	\end{equation}
	Thus we obtain \eqref{y60}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
Let $T$ be a sublinear operator. Given measurable sets $A$ and $B\supset A$ we denote
	\begin{equation}\label{y17}
		\Delta(A,B)=\Delta_T(A,B)=\sup_{x\in A,\, f\neq 0\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})}\frac{\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^{*}\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}}{\langle f\rangle_{B^{*}}}.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{L19}
	If $T$ is an arbitrary sublinear operator, then for any balls $A$, $B$ and $C$ satisfying $A\subset B\subset C$ we have
	\begin{equation}\label{a63}
		\Delta(A,B)\le\Delta(A,C).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Let $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$, $A\subset B\subset C$ and $x\in B$. We have
	\begin{align}
		\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*\setminus A^*})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&=	\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*\setminus A^*}\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^*\setminus A^*})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le \Delta(A,C)\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*\setminus A^*}\rangle_{C^*}\le \Delta(A,C)\langle f\rangle_{B^*}
	\end{align}
that completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{L17}
	If a sublinear operator $T$ satisfies T0) and T1)-conditions, then for any $\gamma$-balls $A,B$ and $C$, satisfying $A\subset B\subset C$, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{h51}
		\Delta(A,C)\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\Delta(A,B)\right).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
First consider the case when $A=B$ and so $\Delta(A,B)=0$. Applying \eqref{y57} with 
	\begin{equation}
		\lambda= \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0\cdot \left(\frac{2\mu(C^*)}{\mu(B)}\right)^\rho\langle f\rangle_{C^*}= \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0\cdot \left(\frac{2}{\mu(B)}\right)^\rho \left(\int_{C^*}\|f|\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{ \varrho}
	\end{equation}
	 we get
	\begin{align*}
		&\mu\left\{x\in B:\, \|T(f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}> \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0\cdot \left(\frac{2\mu(C^*)}{\mu(B)}\right)^\rho\langle f\rangle_{C^*}\right\}\le \frac{\mu(B)}{2}
	\end{align*}	
	and so we find a point $x_0\in B$ such that
	\begin{align}
		\|T(f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})\}(x_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\le  \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0\cdot \left(\frac{2\mu(C^*)}{\mu(B)}\right)^\rho\langle f\rangle_{C^*}\\
		&\lesssim  \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0\cdot \left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^\rho\langle f\rangle_{C^*}.\label{f1}
	\end{align}
	According to T1)-condition and Lemma \ref{L14}, for any $x\in B$ we also have 
	\begin{equation}\label{f2}
		\|T(f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})(x)-T(f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})(x_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1 \langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}} \rangle^*_{B}.
	\end{equation}
	By the definition of $\langle f \rangle^*_{B}$ there is a ball $G\supset B$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{y20}
		\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}} \rangle^*_{B}<2\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{G}.
	\end{equation}
	If $\mu(G)\le \mu(C)$, then we have $G\subset C^{*}$ and therefore 
	\begin{align}
		\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{G}\le \left(\frac{\mu(C^{*})}{\mu(G)}\right)^{\rho}\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{C^{*}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho}\cdot \langle f\rangle_{C^{*}}.\label{h34}
	\end{align}
	If $\mu(G)> \mu(C)$, then $C^*\subset G^{**}$. Hence we get
	\begin{align}
		\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{G}&\le \left(\frac{\mu(G^{**})}{\mu(G)}\right)^{\rho}\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{G^{**}}\\
		&\lesssim\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}}\rangle_{C^{*}}\le \langle f\rangle_{C^{*}}.\label{h35}
	\end{align}
	The combination of \eqref{y20}, \eqref{h34} and \eqref{h35} imply the inequality 
	\begin{equation*}
		\langle f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}} \rangle^*_{B}\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho}\cdot \langle f\rangle_{C^{*}},
	\end{equation*}
	which together with \eqref{f1} and \eqref{f2} gives
	\begin{equation*}
		\|T(f\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(A)}\right)^{\rho} (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1)\langle f\rangle_{C^{*}},\quad x\in A.
	\end{equation*}
The latter implies
\begin{equation*}
	\Delta(B,C)\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho}(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1)
\end{equation*}
that is \eqref{h51} if $A=B$. Now let $A$ be arbitrary.  Thus, for $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and $x\in A$ we have
	\begin{align*}
		\|T(f\cdot &\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{C^{*}\setminus B^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^{*}\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\lesssim\Delta(B,C)\langle f\rangle_{C^{*}}+\Delta(A,B) \langle f\rangle_{B^{*}}\\
		&\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho}(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+ \gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1)\langle f\rangle_{C^{*}}\\
		&\qquad +\left(\frac{\mu(C^{*})}{\mu(B^{*})}\right)^{\rho}\Delta(A,B) \langle f\rangle_{C^{*}}\\
		&\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\rho }(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1+ \Delta(A,B))\langle f\rangle_{C^{*}},
	\end{align*}
	which is the full version of \eqref{h51}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
We will often use the following particular case of inequality \eqref{h51}, that is 
		\begin{equation}\label{y22}
			\Delta(A,B)\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)}\right)^{\rho}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+ \gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\right).
		\end{equation}
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{L15}
	If $T$ is a ${\rm BO}$ operator, then for any $\gamma$-ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ ($A^*\neq X$) there exists a ball $B\supsetneq A$ such that 
		\begin{equation}\label{y62}
			\Delta(A,B)\lesssim \gamma^{\rho}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\gamma^{2\rho}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2,
		\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Let $A$ be a $\gamma$-ball that is $A_1\subset A\subset A_2$, $\mu(A_2)\le \gamma \mu(A_1)$ for some balls $A_1,A_2$.  If $A=A_2$, then by T2) we will have \eqref{y62} with a constant $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2$. If $A\neq A_2$, then, applying \eqref{y22} we obtain
	\begin{equation}
\Delta(A,A_2)\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu(A_2)}{\mu(A)}\right)^{\rho}\left(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+ \gamma^\rho\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\right)\le \gamma^{\rho}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\gamma^{2\rho}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1.
	\end{equation}
Thus we get \eqref{y62} with $B=A_2$.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
\label{R1}
	Taking into account Lemmas \ref{L14} and \ref{L15}, in the definition of ${\rm BO}$ operators we can equivalently consider $\lambda$-balls instead of real balls.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{L16}
	Let $T$ is a ${\rm BO}$ operator and $A$ be a ball. Then for any ball $B\supsetneq A$ such that $\mu(B)\le 2\phi(A)$ we have
	\begin{equation}
		\Delta(A,B)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let balls $B\supsetneq A$ satisfy the conditions of lemma.  Using T2)-condition, we find a ball $C\supsetneq A$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{t6}
	\Delta(A,C)\le\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T).
	\end{equation}
If $\mu(C)>\mu(B)$, then we have $B\subset C^{*}$. Thus, applying Lemmas \ref {L19} and \ref{L17}, we obtain 
\begin{align}
	\Delta(A,B)\le \Delta(A,C^*)&\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\Delta(A,C)\\
	&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T).\label{t8}
\end{align}
	In the case $\mu(C)\le \mu(B)$ we have $C\subset B^{*}$, and since $C\supsetneq A$, we obtain 
	\begin{equation*}
		\mu(B)\gtrsim \mu(B^{*})\ge \mu(C)\ge \phi(A) \ge \frac{\mu(B)}{2}.
	\end{equation*} 
	Thus, once again applying Lemmas \ref {L19}, \ref{L17} and \eqref{t6}, we conclude 
	\begin{align}
			\Delta(A,B)&\le 	\Delta(A,B^*)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+	\Delta(A,C)\\
			&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T).
	\end{align}
This completes the proof of lemma.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{L18}
	Let $T$ be a ${\rm BO}$ operator. For any balls $A$ and $B$ such that $A\cap B\neq \varnothing$ and $\mu(A)<\mu(B)$ there exists a ball $\tilde A$ such that $A\subsetneq \tilde A$, $\tilde A^*\subset B^*$ and 
	\begin{equation}\label{y23}
		\Delta(A,\tilde A)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2
	\end{equation}
	One can also write $A^*$ and $\tilde A^*$ in \eqref{y23} instead of $A$ and $\tilde A$ respectively.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Let a pair $(G,A)$ of balls $G\supsetneq A$ satisfy T2) condition, that is $\Delta(A,G)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2$. If for a hull-ball $[G]$ we have $\mu([G])\le \mu(B)$, then $G^*\subset [G]\subset B^*$ and one can choose $\tilde A=G$ in \eqref{y23}. In the case of $\mu([G])>\mu(B)$ we have $B\subset [G]^{*}$. By Lemma \ref{L17} the pair $(A, [G]^{*})$ also satisfies T2)-condition, and according to Lemma \ref{L19}, so we have for $(A,B)$. Hence we can choose $\tilde A=B$. One can check that for both cases we will have \eqref{y23}. The second part of the lemma may be proved similarly.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{T5-1}
	If a sublinear operator $T$ satisfies T0) and T1)-conditions, then 
	\begin{equation}\label{y14}
		\mu\{x\in X:\, T^*f(x)>\lambda,\, \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)< \delta \lambda\}\lesssim \mu\{x\in X:\, \|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>\lambda/2\}
	\end{equation}
	with a constant $\delta\sim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T))^{-1}$.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Denote by $E=E_\lambda$ the set on the left of \eqref{y14}, which can also be non-measurable. By the definition of $T^*$ for any $x\in E$ there is a ball $B(x)\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{h27}
		x\in B(x),\quad \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}(x)})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>\lambda.
	\end{equation}
	One can check that $E\subset \cup_{x\in E}B(x)$ and
	\begin{equation}\label{y15}
		\langle f\rangle_{B(x)}^*\le \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)<\delta \lambda,\quad x\in E.
	\end{equation}
	Given ball $G$ apply Lemma \ref{L1-1}, we find a sequence $x_k\in E$ such that the balls $\{B_k=B(x_k)\}$ are pairwise disjoint and 
	\begin{equation}\label{h30}
		E\cap G \subset \cup_kB^{*}_k=Q(G).
	\end{equation}
	Since $T$ satisfies T1)-condition, by \eqref{y15} we obtain 
	\begin{equation*}
		\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x_k)-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\cdot \langle f \rangle^*_{B_k}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\delta\lambda,\quad x\in B_k. 
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, we conclude from \eqref{h27} that
	\begin{align}
		\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\ge \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x_k)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\label{h29}\\
		&\qquad -\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x_k)-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}_k})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V} \\
		&\ge \lambda(1-\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\delta ),\quad x\in B_k.
	\end{align}
	For a $\beta>0$ we define measurable sets
	\begin{equation}\label{h28}
		\tilde B_k= \{x\in B_k:\, \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B_k^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \beta\cdot \langle f \rangle^*_{B_k,r}\}.
	\end{equation} 
	Using T0)-condition, the measure of the complement of $\tilde B_k$ is estimated by
	\begin{align*}
		\mu(\tilde B_k^c)&\le \frac{ ( \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0)^{1/\rho}}{(\beta \cdot \langle f \rangle^*_{B_k,r})^{1/\rho}}\cdot \left(\int_{B_k^{*}}|f|^r\right)^{ \varrho/\rho}\le \left(\frac{ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0}{\beta}\right)^{1/\rho}\mu(B_k^{*}) \\
		&\lesssim \left(\frac{ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0}{\beta}\right)^{1/\rho}\mu(B_k).
	\end{align*}
	Using also \eqref{y15}, for an appropriate constant $\beta\sim  \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0$ we have
	\begin{align}
	&	\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B_k^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \beta \delta\lambda,\quad x\in \tilde B_k,\label{y21}\\
	&	\mu(\tilde B_k)\ge\mu(B_k)-\mu(\tilde B_k^c) \ge \mu(B_k)/2.\label{z43}
	\end{align}
	If $x\in \tilde B_k$, then, using sublinearity of $T$ together with relations \eqref{h29} and \eqref{y21}, we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\gtrsim \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B_k^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}-\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B_k^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\ge \lambda(1-\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\delta-\beta\delta)\ge \lambda/2,   	
	\end{align*} 
	where the last inequality can be satisfied for $\delta= 1/2(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1+\beta)\sim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0)^{-1}$.
	Hence we conclude $\cup_k\tilde B_k\subset \{\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>\lambda/2\}$
	and taking into account also \eqref{h30} and \eqref{z43}, we get
	\begin{align*}
		\mu(E\cap G)\le \mu(Q(G))&\le \sum_k\mu(B_k^{*})\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}\cdot \sum_k\mu(B_k)\\
		&\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\cdot  \sum_k\mu(\tilde B_k)\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal K}\mu \{\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>\lambda/2\}.
	\end{align*}
	Since $G$ is arbitrary, applying Lemma \ref{L5} we complete the proof.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{T6}
	If $T$ is a ${\rm BO}$ operator, then $T^*$ is also a ${\rm BO}$ operator. Moreover, we have
	\begin{align}
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T^*)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T),\label{y59}\\
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T^*)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T),\label{y78}\\
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T^*)=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T),		
	\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Weak type inequality \eqref{y57} or condition \eqref{y59} easily follows from Theorems \ref{T1-1} and \ref{T5-1}.	One can also check that for any balls $A,B$ satisfying $A\subset B$ we have 
	\begin{equation*}
		\Delta_{T^*}(A,B)=\Delta_{T}(A,B).
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, if balls $A$ and $B$ satisfy T2)-condition for the operator $T$, then the same conditions hold also for $T^*$ with $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T^*)=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)$. 
	To prove \eqref{y78}, let $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ satisfy 
	\begin{equation}\label{a67}
		{\rm supp\,} f\in X\setminus B^{*}.
	\end{equation}
	Choose arbitrary points $x,x'\in B$ and let estimate $|T^*f(x)- T^*f(x')|$. If $T^*f(x)= T^*f(x')$, then the estimation is trivial. So we can suppose that $T^*f(x)> T^*f(x')$. Using the definition of $T^*f(x)$, we find a ball $A\ni x$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{h37}
		\frac{T^*f(x)+T^*f(x')}{2}< \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}.
	\end{equation} 
First suppose that $\mu(A^*)>\mu(B)$. So we can say that $x'\in B\subset A^{**}\subset A'$, where  $A'$ is a ball satisfying $\mu(A')\le \ensuremath{\mathcal K}^2\mu(A)$. By definition of $T^*$ we can write
\begin{equation}
	T^*f(x')\ge \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^{*}})(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V},
\end{equation}
which together with \eqref{h37} yields 
\begin{align}
	|T^*f(x)-T^*f(x')|&=T^*f(x)-T^*f(x')\\
	&< 2\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}-2T^*f(x')\\
	&\le 2\bigg(\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}-\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^{*}})(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\bigg).\label{a6}
\end{align}
Besides, since $x\in A$, from \eqref{y22} (or Lemma \ref{L17}) and \eqref{a67} it follows that
\begin{align}
	\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A'^*\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\le \Delta(A,A') \langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A'^*\setminus A^{*}}\rangle_{A'^*}\\
&\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu(A')}{\mu(A)}\right)^{\rho}(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)) \langle f \rangle_{A'^*},\\
	&\lesssim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)) \langle f \rangle^*_{B},\label{a81}
\end{align}
Thus, applying the T1)-condition for $T$, from \eqref{a81} and \eqref{a6} we conclude
\begin{align*}
	|T^*f(x)-T^*f(x')|&\le 2\bigg(\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^*})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A'^{*}\setminus A^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\qquad -\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^*})(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\bigg)\\
	&\lesssim \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^*})(x)-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A'^*})(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\qquad +(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T))\langle f \rangle^*_{B} \\
	&\lesssim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)) \langle f \rangle^*_{B}
\end{align*}
that implies \eqref{y78}.  
Now suppose $\mu(A^*)\le \mu(B)$ and therefore $A^*\subset B^*$. Then by \eqref{a67} we get $f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus A^{*}}=f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}}$. Thus, using the same argument, we can write
\begin{align}
	|T^*f(x)-T^*f(x')|&=T^*f(x)-T^*f(x')\\
	&\le 2\bigg(\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}-\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^{*}})(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\bigg)\\
	&\le  \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\langle f \rangle^*_{B}
\end{align}
that again gives \eqref{y78}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{T16}
 If $\{T_\alpha\}$ is a family of ${\rm BO}$ operators with uniformly bounded characteristic constants $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_j(T_\alpha)$, then the maximal operator
 \begin{equation}\label{aa4}
 	Tf(x)=\sup_\alpha \|T_\alpha f(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}
 \end{equation} 
satisfies the bounds
	\begin{align}
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\lesssim \sup_\alpha \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha),\label{aa5}\\
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)\lesssim \sup_\alpha(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T_\alpha)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T_\alpha)).\label{aa6}
	\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Let $A\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be an arbitrary ball and  ${\rm supp\,} f\subset X\setminus A^{*}$.
	Take arbitrary points $x,x'\in A$ and suppose that $Tf(x)\ge Tf(x')$.
	According to the definition of $T$, for any $\delta> 0$ there exists an index $\alpha_0$ such that $Tf(x)\le \|T_{\alpha_0}f(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\delta$.
	On the other hand for the same $\alpha_0$ we have $Tf(x')\ge \|T_{\alpha_0} f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}$. Thus, applying T1) property of $T_{\alpha_0}$, we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		|Tf(x)-Tf(x')|&=Tf(x)-Tf(x')\\
		&\le \|T_{\alpha_0} f(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\delta-\|T_{\alpha_0} f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le \|T_{\alpha_0} f(x)-T_{\alpha_0}f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\delta\\
		&\le \sup_\alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\cdot \langle f \rangle^*_{A}+\delta.
	\end{align*}
	Since $\delta>0$ can be arbitrarily small, we get \eqref{aa5}. \eqref{aa6} follows from Lemma \ref{L16}, according which we can choose a ball $B$ independent of the operators $T_\alpha$ such that 
	\begin{equation}
			\Delta_{T_\alpha}(A,B)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T_\alpha)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T_\alpha)
	\end{equation}
for any $\alpha$. This implies \eqref{aa6}, completing the proof of theorem.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
If $\{T_\alpha\}$ is a family of ${\rm BO}$ operators with uniformly bounded characteristic constants $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_j(T_\alpha)$ and the maximal operator $T$ in \eqref{aa4} satisfies condition T0), then $T$ is a ${\rm BO}$ operator.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
Let the ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ satisfy the doubling condition. If a sublinear operator $T$ satisfies T0) and T1) conditions, then $T$ satisfies also T2) and so it is ${\rm BO}$ operator. Moreover, 
	\begin{equation}\label{y63}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)\lesssim\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+ \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
We need to check T2)-condition. Let $A$ be an arbitrary ball and $B$ satisfy conditions \eqref{h73}. Then applying \eqref{y22}, we get the bound
	\begin{equation*}
		\Delta(A,B)\lesssim \left(\frac{\mu( B)}{\mu(A)}\right)^{\rho}(\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1)\le \eta^{\rho} (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1),
	\end{equation*}
which implies \eqref{y63}.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Consider the operator 
	\begin{align*}
		\Gamma f(x)=\max\left\{\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V},T^{*}f(x),\ensuremath{\mathcal L}\cdot \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)\right\},
	\end{align*}
where $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)$. As $T$ is a ${\rm BO}$ operator, from Theorems \ref {T1-1} and \ref {T6} it follows that the operator $\Gamma$ satisfies the weak type property and $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(\Gamma)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}$. Thus for every ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ the set
\begin{equation}\label{y75}
	F_A=\left\{x\in X:\, \Gamma (f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A^{**}})(x)> \ensuremath{\mathcal L} \lambda \langle f\rangle_{A^{**}}\right\},\quad \lambda>0,
\end{equation}
has a measure 
\begin{equation}\label{y69}
	\mu(F_A)\lesssim \mu(A)/\lambda^{1/\rho}=\alpha\mu(A).
\end{equation}
We consider the sets $F_A$ for a fixed function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ with ${\rm supp\,} f\subset B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$. Applying Proposition \ref{P11} with an admissible constant $\lambda >0$ and $A_0=B$, we find a sparse collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ satisfying the conditions of Proposition \ref{P11}. Then, applying Lemma \ref{L18},  for each $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ and $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$ we may fix a ball $\tilde G$ such that $G\subsetneq \tilde G$, $\tilde G^*\subset A^*$ and $\Delta(G,\tilde G)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}$. From Lemma \ref{L17} we get $\Delta(G,\tilde G^*)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}$ and therefore for any $x\in G$ we can write
\begin{align}
	\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde G^{**}\setminus G^{**}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&=\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde G^{**}\setminus G^{**}}\cdot\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde G^{**}\setminus G^{*}} )(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\le \Delta(G,\tilde G^*)\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde G^{**}\setminus G^{**}}\rangle_{\tilde G^{**}}\\
	&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L} \langle f\rangle_{\tilde G^{**}}, \quad x\in G.\label{y76}
\end{align}
