Having a1) from Proposition \ref{P11}, we can apply Proposition \ref{L13} to the tree-collection  $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ with $\{E_A=A\setminus F_A\}$. Then we get a martingale-collection $\{\bar A:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}\}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ such that $A\setminus F_A$ are pairwise disjoint and \eqref{y67}-\eqref{y64} hold. Moreover, \eqref{y64} may be written in the form
\begin{equation}
	A_0\subset_{a.e.}\bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}( A\setminus F_A)=\bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}(\bar A\setminus F_A),
\end{equation}
where the first inclusion follows from condition a2) of Proposition \ref{P11}. Thus for a.e. $x\in A_0$ one can find a sequence of balls $A_k$, $k=1,2,\ldots,n$ such that $x\in \bar A_n\setminus  F_{A_n}$ and $A_{j+1}\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A_j)$, $j=0,1,\ldots,n-1$. Thus we obtain
\begin{align}
	&	x\in \bar A_n\setminus  F_{A_n}\subset \bar A_{j},\quad j=0,1,\ldots,n-1,\label{y77}\\
	&A_{j+1}^{*}\subset \tilde A_{j+1}^{*}\subset A_{j}^{*},\quad j=0,1,\ldots,n-1.
\end{align}
By a4) we have $\tilde G\setminus F_A\neq \varnothing $ whenever $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. So we can fix  points $\xi_j\in \tilde A_{j+1}\setminus F_{A_{j}},\quad j=0,1,\ldots,n-1$. 
Applying T1) and T2) properties (see also Remark \ref{R1}) and the definition of $F_A$ in \eqref{y75}, we get inequalities
\begin{align}
&\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(\xi_j)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
&\qquad\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1\langle f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}}\rangle_{{\tilde A_{j+1}^{*}}}^*\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L} \ensuremath{\mathcal M}(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{j}^{**}})(\xi_j)\\
&\qquad \le \Gamma(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{j}^{**}})(\xi_j)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}  \langle f\rangle_{A_j^{**}},\label{a75}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
	\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(\xi_j)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le T^{*}(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}})(\xi_j)\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}  \langle f\rangle_{A_{j}^{**}},\label{a105}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
	\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde A_{j+1}^{**}\setminus A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L} \langle f\rangle_{\tilde A_{j+1}^{**}}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L} \ensuremath{\mathcal M}(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{j}^{**}})(\xi_j)\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}  \langle f\rangle_{A_j^{**}},\label{a76}
\end{align}
where in \eqref{a76} we also use \eqref{y76}. Thus, applying  \eqref{a75}, \eqref{a105} and  \eqref{a76}, we conclude	
\begin{align}
	\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}	&\le\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\le \left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(\xi_j)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\qquad+\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_j^{**}\setminus \tilde A_{j+1}^{**}})(\xi_j)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\qquad+\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\tilde A_{j+1}^{**}\setminus A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
	&\le C \ensuremath{\mathcal L}\cdot\langle f\rangle_{A_j^{**}}+\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{j+1}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\label{y24}
\end{align}
with an admissible constant $C>0$. Since $x\in A_n\setminus  F_{A_n}$, we can  also write $\left\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_{n}^{**}})(x)\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}  \langle f\rangle_{A_n^{**}}$. Finally, using \eqref{y77} and the iteration of \eqref{y24}, we conclude
\begin{align}
		\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}=\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{A_0^{**}})(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}\sum_{j=0}^n \langle f\rangle_{A_j^{**}}\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}\sum_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}} \langle f\rangle_{[[A]]}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar A}(x).
\end{align}
This gives the required sparse domination \eqref{y51} with respect to the sparse family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{[[A]]:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}\}$ and the martingale-collection $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{A:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}\}$. To show \eqref{y53} we use \eqref{s27} that holds for the initial family $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$.  Indeed, from \eqref{s27} and the definition of martingale-collection it follows that
\begin{align}
	&\sum_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}^{n}(A_0)}\mu(\bar G)\lesssim \alpha^n \mu(A_0),\\
	&\bigcup_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}^{n+1}(A_0)}\bar G\subset \bigcup_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}^{n}(A_0)}\bar G
\end{align}
that easily implies \eqref{y53} choosing a small enough $\alpha$. Condition \eqref{y52} holds with a ball $B'=[[[A_0]]]$. Applying Theorem \ref{T6}, we derive the sparse domination \eqref{y51} for the truncated operator $T^*$ too. Theorem is proved.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
\label{D2}
	For $f\in L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ let $M_f(B)\subset B$ be the union of all measurable sets $E\subset B$, satisfying 
	\begin{equation}\label{y99}
		\mu(E)>\mu(B)/2,\quad {\rm OSC}_E(f)\le 2{\rm OSC}_{B,1/2}(f).
	\end{equation}
A vector $m=m_f(B)\in \ensuremath{\mathbb U}$ is said to be a median of the function $f$ on $B$ if there exists a point $x\in M_f(B)$ such that $f(x)=m$.
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{L7}
	For any function $f\in L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ it holds the inequality 
\begin{equation}
{\rm OSC}_{M_f(B)}(f)\le 4{\rm OSC}_{B,1/2}(f).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{T3}
	Let $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be a doubling ball-basis. Then for any measurable function $f\in L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $A_0$ there exist a sparse family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ and a martingale-family of measurable sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that
	\begin{align}
		& \bigcup_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}G\subset A_0^*,\label{y89}\\
		&\mu\big\{x\in X:\,\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x)>\lambda\big\}\lesssim \exp(-c\lambda)\mu(A_0),\quad \lambda>0,\label{y90}\\
		&\|f(x)- m_f(A_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim \sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}{\rm OSC}_{G,\beta}(f)\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x)\text{ for a.e. }x\in A_0,\label{y91}
		\end{align}
	where $0<\beta<1$, $c>0$ are admissible constants and $m=m_f(A_0)$ is a median of a function $f$ over the ball $A_0$.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Given $0<\beta<1$.  For any ball $A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ we may fix a set $E_{A}\subset [A]$ such that
		\begin{equation}\label{a13}
			\mu(E_A)\ge \beta\mu([A]),\quad {\rm OSC}_{E_A}(f)\le 2{\rm OSC}_{[A],\beta}(f).
		\end{equation}
	For the sets $F_A=[A]\setminus E_A$ we have $\mu(F_A)\le\alpha \mu([A])\lesssim\alpha \mu(A)$, where $\alpha=1-\beta$. Thus, applying Proposition \ref{P11}, we find a sparse tree-collection of balls $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$, satisfying a1)-a4). Since our ball-basis is doubling, we may also have \eqref{y27} for $G'=G$. Besides, for a small enough $\alpha$ inequality \eqref{s27} may imply $\mu([G])<\mu(A)$ and so we get $[G]\subset A^*\subset [A]$ whenever $G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)$. Hence we can write
	\begin{align}
		&G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow [G]\subset A^*\subset [A],\label{y45}\\
		&G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow\mu(G\cap F_A)<\mu(G)/2 \text { (see \eqref{y27})}.\label{y46}
	\end{align}
Moreover, for an admissible $\alpha$, inequality \eqref{s27} also implies
\begin{equation}\label{y88}
	\sum_{G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)}\mu([G])\le \mu([A])/2.
\end{equation}
According to a2) we have
\begin{equation}\label{y48}
A_0\subset_{a.e.} \bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}A\cap E_{A}\subset \bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}} E_{A}.
\end{equation}
	 We also claim for a smaller enough $\alpha$ that 
	\begin{equation}\label{a17}
		G\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A)\Rightarrow E_A\cap E_G\neq\varnothing.
	\end{equation} 
	Indeed, by \eqref{y45} and \eqref{y46} we have $\mu(G\cap E_A)>\mu(G)/2$.
	Thus, using also \eqref{a13}, we get
	\begin{align}
		\mu(E_A\cap E_G)&\ge \mu((E_A\cap G)\cap (E_G\cap G))\\
		&=\mu(E_A\cap G)-\mu((E_A\cap G)\setminus (E_G\cap G))\\
		&=\mu(E_A\cap G)-\mu(G\setminus (E_G\cap G))\\
		&\ge \mu(G)/2-\mu([G]\setminus E_G)\\
		&\ge \mu(G)/2-\alpha\mu([G])\\
		&\ge \mu(G)(1/2-\alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal K})>0,
	\end{align}
	and so \eqref{a17} follows. 	
	According to \eqref{y45} the tree-collection of measurable sets $\ensuremath{\mathscr A}=\{[A]:\, A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}\}$ satisfies \eqref{y44}. Thus, applying Proposition \ref{L13} and \eqref{y48}, we find a martingale family of measurable sets $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}\Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$ such that
	\begin{equation}
		\bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}\bar A\cap E_A=\bigcup_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}E_A\supset _{a.e.} A_0 \text{ (see \eqref{y64})}.
	\end{equation}
	Thus for a.e. $x\in A_0$ we have $x\in \bar A \cap E_A$ for some $A\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}$. Fix a unique chain sequence of balls $A_0, A_1,\ldots ,A_n=A$ such that  $A_{j+1}\in {\mathfrak {Ch}}(A_j)$, $j=0,1,\ldots,n-1$. According to the martingale property of $\bar \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, the relation $x\in \bar A=\bar A_n$ implies 
	\begin{equation}\label{y35}
		x\in \bigcap_{j=0}^n\bar A_j.
	\end{equation}
	Using \eqref{a17}, we find points $\xi_j\in E_{A_{j-1}}\cap E_{A_{j}}$, $j=1,2,\ldots, n-1$. Set also $\xi_n=x$. Since $\xi_{j},\xi_{j+1}\in E_{A_j}$, from \eqref{a13} it follows that
	\begin{equation}\label{a28}
		\|f(\xi_{j})-f(\xi_{j+1})\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\le 2{\rm OSC}_{[A_j],\beta}(f),\quad j=1,2,\ldots, n-1.
	\end{equation} 
	In addition, if $\beta>1/2$ from \eqref{a13}  it follows that $E_{A_0}\subset M_f(A_0)$(see Definition \ref{D2}). Then, since $\xi_1\in E_{A_0}$, by Lemma \ref{L7} we can write
	\begin{equation}\label{a49}
		\|f(\xi_{1})-m_f(A_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\le 4{\rm OSC}_{[A_0],1/2}(f)\le 4{\rm OSC}_{[A_0],\beta}(f).
	\end{equation} 
Applying, \eqref{y35}, \eqref{a28} and \eqref{a49}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\|f(x)-m_f(A_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}&=\|f(\xi_n)-m_f(A_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\le \|f(\xi_1)-m_f(A_0)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\|f(\xi_j)-f(\xi_{j+1})\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\le 4\sum_{j=0}^{n-1} {\rm OSC}_{[A_j],\beta}(f)\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar A_j}(x)\\
		&\le 4\sum_{A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}} {\rm OSC}_{A,\beta}(f)\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar A}(x),
	\end{align}
which implies \eqref{y91}. Relation \eqref{y89} immediately follows from \eqref{y45} and \eqref{y88} implies \eqref{y90}. This completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{L6}
	For any function $f\in L^1_{loc}(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and balls $A\subset B$ we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\|f_A-f_B\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\le \left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)} \right)^{1/r}\langle f\rangle_{\#,B}.
	\end{equation*}
	\begin{proof}
		We have
		\begin{align}
			\left\|f_{A}-f_{B}\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}&\le \frac{1}{\mu(A)}\int_{A}\|f-f_{B}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
			&\le\left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)} \right)^{1/r}\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}\|f-f_{B}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{1/r}\le \left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)} \right)^{1/r}\langle f\rangle_{\#,B}.
		\end{align}
	\end{proof}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
We have
		\begin{align}
			\left\|f_{A}-f_{B}\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}&\le \frac{1}{\mu(A)}\int_{A}\|f-f_{B}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
			&\le\left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)} \right)^{1/r}\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B}\|f-f_{B}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{1/r}\le \left(\frac{\mu(B)}{\mu(A)} \right)^{1/r}\langle f\rangle_{\#,B}.
		\end{align}
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{L10}
	Let the ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ satisfy the doubling condition. Then for any $f\in L^1_{loc}(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and balls $A\subset B$ it holds the inequality
	\begin{equation*}
		\left\|f_A-f_{B}\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim \log(1+\mu(B)/\mu(A))\cdot \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
According to Lemma \ref{L1}, we may find a sequence of balls $A=A_0\subset A_1\subset \ldots\subset A_n=[B]$ satisfying such that $\mu(A_{k+1})\lesssim \mu(A_k)$. One can easily check that $n\lesssim \log \left(1+\mu(B)/\mu(A)\right)$.
By Lemma \ref{L6} we have
	\begin{equation}
		\left\|f_{A_k}-f_{A_{k+1}}\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim \langle f\rangle_{A_{k+1},\#}\le \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*.
	\end{equation}
	Thus we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		\|f_A-f_B\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}&\le \sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\|f_{A_k}-f_{A_{k+1}}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\|f_{B}-f_{[B]}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim n\langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*\\
		&\lesssim \log(1+\mu(B)/\mu(A))\langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Let $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$. Applying restriction condition R5) we find a ball $B'\supset B$ such that 
	\begin{equation}
	\sup_\alpha {\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B''}))<\delta
	\end{equation}
for every ball $B''\supset B'$. Then let $G_n$ be the sequence of balls generated from Lemma \ref{L5}. We have $\langle f\rangle_{G_n}^*\to 0$ and so there is a ball $G=G_n\supset B'^*$ such that $\langle f\rangle^*_{G} <\delta$.
Applying restriction condition R5), we can write
	\begin{align}
	&B^*\subset G,\\
	&\sup_\alpha {\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{G}))<\delta,\label{y86}\\
	&\sup_\alpha{\rm OSC}_{B}(T_\alpha(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus G}))\le \sup_\alpha \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\cdot \langle f\rangle^*_{G}<\delta \sup_\alpha\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha). \label{y85}
	\end{align}
	Then we can write 
		\begin{align*}
		f&=f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus G}+(f-f_B)\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*}+(f-f_B)\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{G\setminus B^*}+f_B\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{G}\\
		&=f_0+f_1+f_2+f_3.
	\end{align*}
From \eqref{y86}, \eqref{y85} we can obtain
\begin{equation}\label{x2}
	\sup_\alpha {\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(f_0))<\varepsilon,\quad \sup_\alpha {\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(f_3))<\varepsilon
\end{equation}
with an arbitrary small $\varepsilon>0$. Consider the set $E_{B}=\{y\in B:\, Tf_1(y)\le \lambda\}$. According to the $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0$-condition of the maximal operator $T$ for 
	 \begin{align}
	 	\lambda=\ensuremath{\mathcal K}^{1/r}(1-\beta)^{-1/r}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)\langle f_1\rangle_{B^*}
	 \end{align}
	 we have
	\begin{equation}
		\mu(B\setminus E_{B})=\mu\{y\in B:\, Tf_1(y)>\lambda\}\le\frac{(1-\beta )\mu(B^*)}{\ensuremath{\mathcal K}}\le  (1-\beta )\mu(B).
	\end{equation}
	Thus, applying also Lemma \ref{L6}, we can write
	\begin{align}
		&\mu(E_{B})>\beta\mu(B),\\
		& Tf_1(y)\lesssim(1-\beta)^{-1/r}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)\langle f-f_B\rangle_{B^*}\\
		&\qquad \quad\le (1-\beta)^{-1/r}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)(\langle f-f_{B^*}\rangle_{B^*}+\|f_B-f_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U})\\
		&\qquad \quad\lesssim(1-\beta)^{-1/r} \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}\text{ for every }y\in E_B.\label{y87}
	\end{align}
	Now choose two arbitrary points $x,x'\in E_B$ and suppose $Tf(x)\ge Tf(x')$. Then for some $\alpha$ we have
	\begin{align}
		|Tf(x)-Tf(x')|=Tf(x)-Tf(x')\le 2( \|T_\alpha f(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}-\|T_\alpha f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}).
	\end{align}
	On the other hand, using \eqref{y87}, for small enough $\varepsilon$ in \eqref{x2} we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\|T_\alpha f(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}-&\|T_\alpha f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le \|T_\alpha f(x)-T_\alpha f(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le {\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(f_0))+{\rm OSC}_B(T_\alpha(f_3))\\
		&\qquad +\|T_\alpha f_1(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T_\alpha f_1(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T_\alpha f_2(x)-T_\alpha f_2(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\le 2\varepsilon +\|T f_1(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T f_1(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}+\|T_\alpha f_2(x)-T_\alpha f_2(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\\
		&\lesssim (1-\beta)^{-1/r}\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}\\
		&\qquad +\|T_\alpha f_2(x)-T_\alpha f_2(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}.\label{y28}
	\end{align}
	Applying T1)-condition, we get
	\begin{align}
	\|T_\alpha f_2(x)-T_\alpha f_2(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\le {\rm OSC}_{B}(T_\alpha (f_2))\\
	&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\sup_{C\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}\,C\supset B}\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(C)}{\mu(B)}\right)\langle f_2\rangle_{C}\\
	&\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\langle f_2\rangle_{A}\label{x10}
	\end{align} 
	for some ball $A\supset B$.
	Clearly we can suppose that $\mu(A)>2\mu(B)$, since otherwise we would have $A\subset B^*$ that means $\langle f_2\rangle_{A,r}=0$ and so the left hand side of \eqref{x10} is zero. Thus, applying Lemma \ref{L10}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\|T_\alpha f_2(x)-T_\alpha f_2(x')\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}&\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\langle f-f_B\rangle_{A}\\
		&\le 2\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)(\langle f-f_A\rangle_{A} +|f_A-f_B|)\\
		&\lesssim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\log\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}\\
		&=\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T_\alpha)\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}.\label{y29}
	\end{align}
	Thus, combining \eqref{y28} and \eqref{y29}, we obtain \eqref{y30}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Let $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and $B$ be a ball.  Applying Proposition \ref{T3} to the function $T(f)$ with $A_0=B$, we find sparse and martingale families of balls $\bar\ensuremath{\mathscr A} \Subset \ensuremath{\mathscr A}$, satisfying \eqref{y89}, \eqref{y90} and the bound
	\begin{equation}\label{y92}
		|Tf(x)- m_{T(f)}(B)|\lesssim \sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathscr A}}{\rm OSC}_{G,\beta}(T(f))\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\bar G}(x)\text{ for a.e. }x\in B
	\end{equation}
	Combining  \eqref{y92} with the oscillation estimate in \eqref{y30}, we get \eqref{y94}. Then \eqref{y89} $\Rightarrow$ \eqref{y52} and \eqref{y90} $\Rightarrow$ \eqref{y53}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- definition-15: body_md -->
Let $f,g\in L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ be measurable functions.  The function  $f$ is said to be strongly dominated by $g$ if for any $0<\alpha<1$ there exists a number $\beta=c(\alpha)>0$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{01}
		{\rm OSC}_{B,\alpha}(f)< \beta\cdot{\rm INF}_{B}(g).
	\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-15 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
If the ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ in a measure space $(X,\mu)$ is doubling and measurable functions $f,g\in L^0(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$  satisfy strong domination condition \eqref{01}, then for any ball $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ we have
	\begin{equation}\label{6}
		\mu\{x\in B:\,\|f(x)-m_f(B)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}>\lambda  \|g(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\}\lesssim\exp(-c\cdot\lambda )\mu(B),\quad \lambda>0,
	\end{equation}
	where $c>0$ is an admissible constant and $m_f(B)$ is a median of $f$ on the ball $B$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- corollary-5: body_md -->
\label{C8}
	Let $(X,\mu)$ be measure space equipped with a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ (which can also be non-doubling) and $T$ be a general ${\rm BO}$-operator. Then the inequality
	\begin{equation}\label{a44}
		\mu\{x\in B:\,\|Tf(x)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>t \cdot \ensuremath{\mathcal M} f(x)|\}\lesssim \exp (-t/c(T))\mu(B),\quad t>0,
	\end{equation}
	holds for any function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B$, where $c(T)\sim \ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)$.
<!-- end corollary-5 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{C5}
	Under the assumptions of Theorem \ref{T8} for any $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and a ball $B$ the maximal operator $Tf=\sup_\alpha|T_\alpha f|$
	satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{x3}
		\mu\{x\in B:\,|Tf(x)-m_{T(f)}(B)|>t  \ensuremath{\mathcal M}_\# f(x)|\}\lesssim \exp (-ct)\mu(B),\, t>0.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
\label{C4}
	Under the assumptions of Theorem \ref{T8} the maximal operator $Tf=\sup_\alpha|T_\alpha f|$ is bounded on ${\rm BMO }$. More precisely, 
\begin{equation}
\|T(f)\|_{{\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb R})}\lesssim \|f\|_{{\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})}
\end{equation}
for any function
\begin{equation}
	f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})\cap {\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U}).
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
First apply Corollary \ref{C5} for the identical operator $T(f)=f$, which is obviously a restricted type of ${\rm BO}$ operator with parameter $r=1$. We have
	$\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_\#f(x)\le \|f\|_{{\rm BMO }}$ for all $x\in X$, where we suppose $r=1$ in the definition of $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_\#$. Thus, applying \eqref{x3}, we obtain the John-Nirenberg inequality
	\begin{equation}\label{x4}
		\mu\{x\in B:\,\|f(x)-m\|_{\ensuremath{\mathbb U}}>t \cdot\|f\|_{\rm BMO }\}\lesssim \exp (-c\cdot t)\mu(B),\quad t>0,
	\end{equation}
where by a standard argument  $m$ can be also replaced by $f_B$. Returning to the general case of $r\ge 1$ from \eqref{x4} it follows that 
\begin{equation}\label{x5}
	\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_\#f(x)\lesssim \|f\|_{\rm BMO },\quad x\in X.
\end{equation}
Now let $T$ be the operator in Corollary \ref{C5}. From \eqref{x3} and \eqref{x5} we obtain
\begin{equation}
	\mu\{x\in B:\,\|Tf(x)-m_{T(f)}(B)\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}>t \cdot\|f\|_{\rm BMO }\}\lesssim \exp (-c\cdot t)\mu(B),\quad t>0,
\end{equation}
then by the same standard argument we get $\|T(f)\|_{\rm BMO }\lesssim \|f\|_{\rm BMO }$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{T1}
	Every strong-sublinear ${\rm BO}$ operator $T$ boundedly maps $L^\infty$ into ${\rm BMO }$. Moreover,
	\begin{equation*}
		\|T(f)\|_{{\rm BMO }(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb V})}\lesssim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T))\|f\|_{L^\infty(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})}.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Sparse domination \eqref{y51} implies boundedness of $T$ on $L^p$ for any $r<p<\infty$.  Namely we can write
	\begin{equation}\label{x15}
		\|T\|_{L^p(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})\to L^p(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb V})}\lesssim (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T)).
	\end{equation}
	Take a function $f\in L^\infty (X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and for $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ denote
	\begin{equation}\label{x8}
		\alpha_B=\left(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})\right)_B.
	\end{equation}
	Strong-subadditivity of $T$ implies
	\begin{equation*}
		\|Tf-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}\le \|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*})\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}.
	\end{equation*}
	Hence, applying T1)-condition and \eqref{x15}, we get
	\begin{align*}
		\langle Tf-\alpha_B\rangle_{B}&=\left\langle Tf-T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})+T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*})-\alpha_B\right\rangle_{B}\\
		&\le \left\langle T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*})\right\rangle_{B}+{\rm OSC}_{B}(T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{X\setminus B^*}))\\
		&\lesssim \left\langle T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*} \right\rangle_{B}+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\langle f\rangle_B^*\\
		&\lesssim \left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_X\|T(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb V}^p\right)^{1/p}+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\|f\|_\infty\\
		&\le \|T\|_{L^p\to L^p}\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_X\|f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^p\right)^{1/p}+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)\|f\|_\infty\\
		&\le (\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1(T)+\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2(T))\|f\|_\infty.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
If a Calder\'on-Zygmund kernel satisfies logarithmic Dini condition
	\begin{equation}
		[\omega]=\int_0^1\frac{\omega(t)\log(1/t)}{t}dt<\infty,\label{x6}
	\end{equation} 
	then the operators $T_{g}$ satisfy \eqref{y83} uniformly with respect to functions $g\in L^\infty(\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d)$, $\|g\|_\infty\le 1$.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Let $f\in L^r(\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d)$ and $B=B(x_0,R)\subset \ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d$ be an Euclidean ball of center $x_0$ and radius $R$. One can check that $B^*=B(x_0,R^*)$, where $R^*=(1+2\cdot 2^{1/d})R\in [R,5R]$. If any point $x\in B=B(x_0,R)$, using \eqref{x9} and \eqref{x6}, we get
	\begin{align}
		|T_{g}(f\cdot & \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d\setminus B^*})(x)-T_{g}(f\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d\setminus B^*})(x_0)|\\
		&\lesssim \int_{|y-x_0|>R^*}|K(x,y)-K(x_0,y)||f(y)|dy\\
		&\le \sum_{k=0}^\infty\int_{2^{k+1}R^*\ge |y-x_0|>2^kR^*}\omega\left(\frac{|x-x_0|}{|x_0-y|}\right)\cdot \frac{1}{|x_0-y|^d}|f(y)|dy\\
		&\lesssim  \sum_{k=1}^\infty k\omega(2^{-k})\frac{1}{k |B(x_0,2^{k+1}r)|}\int_{B(x_0,2^{k+1}r)}|f(y)|dy\\
		&\lesssim[\omega] \sup_{A\supset B}\log^{-1}\left(1+\frac{\mu(A)}{\mu(B)}\right)\langle f\rangle_{A},
	\end{align}
where $\sup$ is taken over all the balls $A\supset B$. Thus we get R4).
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
If 
	\begin{equation}\label{x7}
		[K]=\sup_{ R,R':\, R'>2R}\,\sup_{t\in B(x,R)}\left|\int_{B(x,R')}K(x,y)-K(t,y)dy\right|<\infty,
	\end{equation} 
	then the  operators $T_{g}$, $\|g\|_\infty\le 1$, satisfy restriction condition R5) uniformly.
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- theorem-11: body_md -->
Let $\{Q_\alpha\}\subset \ensuremath{\mathcal Q}_d$ be a family of polynomials and $K(x,y)$ be a Calder\'on-Zygmund kernel with modulus of continuity $w(t)=t^\delta$, $\delta>0$. If $K$ satisfies \eqref{x7}, then for the operator 
\begin{equation}\label{x14}
	Tf(x)=\sup_{\alpha}\left|\int_{\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d}K(x,y)e^{2\pi i Q_\alpha(y)}f(y)dy\right|
\end{equation}
we have bounds \eqref{y94}, \eqref{y30} and \eqref{x3}.
<!-- end theorem-11 -->
<!-- theorem-12: body_md -->
\label{T13}
	Vector-valued versions of operators \eqref{x11} and \eqref{x12} are ${\rm BO}$ operators with parameters $\varrho=\rho=r=1$ and absolute constants $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_0$, $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_1=0$, $\ensuremath{\mathcal L}_2$. If the ball-basis (martingal filtration) is doubling, then both operators became restricted type ${\rm BO}$ operators.
<!-- end theorem-12 -->
<!-- theorem-13: body_md -->
Operators \eqref{x30} and \eqref{x32} are ${\rm BO}$ operators with parameters 
	\begin{equation}\label{x31}
		\varrho=r=1,\quad \rho=1-\alpha/n.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-13 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
The case of $\alpha=0$ for the maximal operator $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_\alpha$ was proved in \cite{Kar3} and the general case may be proved similarly. So let us consider the operator \eqref{x30}. We need to prove only conditions T0) and T1), since the Euclidean ball-basis is doubling. It is well-known that $I_\alpha$ is of weak $(1,q)$ type with $q$ satisfying $q^{-1}=\rho= 1-\alpha/n$. This implies T0) condition with parameters \eqref{x31}. Then suppose $B=B(x_0,R)$ and $x,x'\in B$, $y\in B^*$, are arbitrary points. One can check that
	\begin{equation}
		\left|\frac{1}{|x-y|^{n-\alpha}}-\frac{1}{|x'-y|^{n-\alpha}}\right|\lesssim \frac{R}{|x-y|^{n+1-\alpha}}.
	\end{equation}
Thus for $f\in L^1(\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d)$ we obtain
\begin{equation}
	|I_\alpha f(x)-I_\alpha f(x')|\lesssim \sum_{k=0}^\infty \frac{1}{2^k}\cdot \frac{1}{|B(x_0,2^kR)|^\rho}\int_{B(x_0,2^kR)}|f(y)|dy\lesssim \langle f\rangle_B^*,
\end{equation}
which implies T1) condition.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- corollary-8: body_md -->
For any function $f\in L^1(\ensuremath{\mathbb R}^d)$ with ${\rm supp\,} f\subset B\in\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ there is a sparse family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathcal S}$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{x35}
		|I_\alpha f(x)|\lesssim C(\alpha)\sum_{G\in \ensuremath{\mathcal S}}\left\langle f\right\rangle_{G}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{G}(x),\text { a.e. } x\in B,
	\end{equation}
where 
\begin{equation}
	\langle f\rangle_G=\frac{1}{|G|^\rho}\int_G|f|,\quad \rho=1-\alpha/d.
\end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-8 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
Let $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ be a family of sets of an arbitrary set $X$. We say a family of balls $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ satisfies the Besicovitch $D$-condition with a constant $D\in \ensuremath{\mathbb N}$, if for any collection ${\mathscr G\,}\subset \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ one can find a subscollection ${\mathscr G\,}'\subset {\mathscr G\,}$ such that
	\begin{align*}
		&\bigcup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}}G= \bigcup_{G\in {\mathscr G\,}'}G,\\
		&\sum_{ G\in {\mathscr G\,}'}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_G(x)\le D.
	\end{align*}
	We say $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ is martingale system if $D=1$.
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- theorem-14: body_md -->
If a ball-basis $\ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ satisfies Besicovitch's condition and $w$ is an $A_{p,q}$ weight with parameters parameters $q>p>1$ satisfying 
	\begin{equation}
		p^{-1}-q^{-1}=1-\rho,
	\end{equation} then the sparse operator \eqref{x16} satisfies the sharp bound
	\begin{equation}\label{x34}
		\|\ensuremath{\mathscr A}\|_{L^q(w^q)\to L^p(w^p)}\lesssim [w]_{A_{p,q}}^{\rho \max\{1,p'/q\}}.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-14 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{LK1}
	If $A\subset B$ are balls and $x\notin B^*$, then $d(x,A)\ge\mu(B)$.
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
Suppose to the contrary that $d(x,A)< \mu(B)$, which means there is a ball $A'\supset A$ such that $x\in A'$ and $\mu(A')<\mu(B)$. Then we get $A'\subset B^*$, which is in contradiction with $x\notin B^*$.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{LK3}
	For any ball $B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}$ and a function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ it holds the inequality
	\begin{equation}\label{a12}
		\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}\le{\rm INF}_{B}(\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_{\#}(f))\lesssim \langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
The proof of the left hand side of the inequality is straightforward. To prove the right hand side inequality, we will need the following standard inequalities
	\begin{align}
		&\langle f\rangle_{\#,B}\le \langle f-c\rangle_B+\|f_B-c\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\le 2\langle f-c\rangle_B,\quad c\in \ensuremath{\mathbb U},\label{a33}\\
		&\langle f\rangle_{\#,B}\le 2\langle f-f_{B^*}\rangle_{B}\le 2\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\int_{B^*}\|f-f_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{1/r}\lesssim \langle f\rangle_{\#,B^*},\label{a35}
	\end{align}
	where $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and $B$ is an arbitrary ball. For any $x\in B$ there exists a ball $B(x)\ni x$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{a36}
		\langle f\rangle_{\#,B(x)}>\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_{\#}f(x)/2\ge {\rm INF}_{B}(\ensuremath{\mathcal M}_{\#}(f))/2=\lambda.
	\end{equation}
	Applying Lemma \ref{L1-1}, we find a sequence of pairwise disjoint balls $\{B_k\}\subset \{B(x):\, x\in B\}$ such that $\cup_kB_k^*\supset B$. If some $B_k$ satisfies $\mu(B_k)>\mu(B)$, then we have $B\subset B_k^*$ and, using \eqref{a35}, we get
	\begin{align*}
		\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}\ge \langle f\rangle_{\#,B_k^*}&\gtrsim\langle f\rangle_{\#,B_k}>\lambda.
	\end{align*}
	If $\mu(B_k)\le \mu(B)$ for every $k$, then  $\cup_kB_k\subset B^*$. Therefore by \eqref{a33}, \eqref{a36} and the pairwise disjointness of $B_k$ we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}&\ge \langle f\rangle_{\#,B^*}\ge \left(\frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\sum_k\int_{B_k}\|f-f_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{1/r}\\
		&\gtrsim \left(\frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\sum_k\int_{B_k}\|f-f_{B_k}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}^r\right)^{1/r}\\
		&=\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\sum_k\mu(B_k)(\langle f\rangle_{\#,B_k})^r\right)^{1/r}\\
		&\ge \lambda\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\sum_k\mu(B_k)\right)^{1/r}\\
		&\gtrsim\lambda\left(\frac{1}{\mu(B)}\sum_k\mu(B_k^*)\right)^{1/r}\ge \lambda,
	\end{align*}
	which gives the right inequality in \eqref{a12}.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{LK2}
	Let $\omega$ satisfy \eqref{y7} and a family $\phi=\{\phi_B:\, B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}\}$ be $\omega$-regular. Then for any function $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ and balls $A\subset B$ we have
	\begin{equation}\label{y2}
		\int_{X}\|f-f_{\phi_A}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\phi_B\lesssim (\|\omega\|+\log(\mu(B)/\mu(A)) )\cdot \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
Applying the doubling condition we find a sequence of balls
	\begin{align}
		&B=B_0\subset B_1\subset \ldots\subset B_n\subset \ldots, \\ 
		&2\mu(B_{n-1})\le \mu(B_{n})\le \eta \mu(B_{n-1}).
	\end{align}
	For $x\in B_{n}^*\setminus B_{n-1}^*$ by Lemma \ref{LK1} we have 
	\begin{equation}
		d(x,B)\ge  \mu(B_{n-1}^*)\gtrsim \mu(B_n^*)\gtrsim 2^n\mu(B).
	\end{equation} 
	Thus, using \eqref{y4}, we obtain
	\begin{equation}
		\phi_B(x)\lesssim \frac{\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B^*}(x)}{\mu(B^*)}+\sum_{n=1}^\infty\frac{\omega(2^{-n})}{\mu(B_n^*)}\ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{B_{n}^*\setminus B_{n-1}^*}(x).
	\end{equation}
	Then by Lemma \ref{L10} we have 
	\begin{align}
		\|f_{B_{n}^*}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}&\le \|f_{B_{n}^*}-f_B\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\|f_{B}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\lesssim (n+\log(\mu(B)/\mu(A)) )\langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*. 
	\end{align}
	Thus we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\int_{X}\|f-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\phi_B&\lesssim \frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\int_{B^*}\|f-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\sum_{n=1}^\infty \frac{\omega(2^{-n})}{\mu(B_n^*)}\int_{B_{n}^*\setminus B_{n-1}^*}\|f-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\le\frac{1}{\mu(B^*)}\int_{B^*}\|f-f_{B^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\sum_{n=1}^\infty \frac{\omega(2^{-n})}{\mu(B_{n}^*)}\int_{B_{n}^*}\|f-f_{B_{n}^*}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\qquad+\|f_{B^*}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\sum_{n=1}^\infty \omega(2^{-n})\|f_{B_{n}^*}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\lesssim (\|\omega\|+\log(\mu(B)/\mu(A)))\cdot  \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*.\label{k5}
	\end{align}
	Applying this inequality with $B=A$ we get
	\begin{equation}\label{k6}
		\|f_{\phi_A}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}=\left\|\int_{X}(f-f_A)\phi_A\right\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim  \|\omega\|\cdot \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*
	\end{equation}
	then, combining \eqref{k5} and \eqref{k6}, we obtain
	\begin{align*}
		\int_{X}\|f-f_{\phi_A}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\phi_B&\le \int_{X}\|f-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\phi_B+\|f_{\phi_A}-f_A\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\\
		&\lesssim (\|\omega\|+\log(\mu(B)/\mu(A)))\cdot \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{RK1}
	We will also use the inequality 
	\begin{equation}\label{k7}
		\|f_{\phi_A}-f_{\phi_B}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\lesssim(\|\omega\|+\log(\mu(B)/\mu(A)) )\cdot \langle f\rangle_{\#,A}^*,
	\end{equation}
	which immediately follows from \eqref{y2}.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
Let $f\in L^r(X,\ensuremath{\mathbb U})$ be a nontrivial function and $B$ be an arbitrary ball. Set $g=(f-f_{\phi_B})\cdot \ensuremath{\textbf 1}_{[B]}$ and  $E=E_{B,\lambda}=\{y\in B:\, \ensuremath{\mathcal M} g(y)\le \lambda\}$, where $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}$ is the maximal function in \eqref{1-1} with parameters $\varrho=\rho=1/r$, $r\ge 1$. According to the weak-$L^r$ bound of $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}$ we have
	\begin{equation}
		\mu(B\setminus E)=\mu\{y\in B:\, \ensuremath{\mathcal M} g(y)>\lambda\}\lesssim  \frac{1}{\lambda^r}\cdot \int_{[B]}|g|^r.
	\end{equation}
	So for an appropriate number $\lambda\sim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\langle g\rangle_{[B]}$	we have $\mu(B\setminus E)< (1-\alpha)\mu(B)$ and therefore,
	\begin{equation}\label{b2}
		\mu(E)>\alpha \mu(B).
	\end{equation} 
	Applying left hand side of \eqref{y4}, as well as \eqref{y2} and \eqref{k7}, for any $y\in E$ we can write
	\begin{align}
		&\ensuremath{\mathcal M} g(y)\lesssim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\langle g\rangle_{[B]}=(1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\langle f-f_{\phi_B}\rangle_{[B]}\label{b1}\\
		&\qquad\quad\lesssim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\left(\langle f-f_{\phi_{[B]}}\rangle_{\phi_{[B]}}+\|f_{\phi_B}-f_{\phi_{[B]}}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}\right)\\
		&\qquad\quad\, \lesssim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r}\|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B},\quad y\in E.
	\end{align}
	Take arbitrary points $x,x'\in E$. Without loss of generality we can suppose that $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)\ge \ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')$. For any $\delta >0$ there is a ball $A\in {\mathscr G\,}(x)$ (so $A\ni x$)  such that
	\begin{equation}\label{k1}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)\le  \langle f\rangle_{A}+\delta.
	\end{equation}
	If $\mu(A)>\mu(B)$, then we have $x'\in B\subset [A]$ and so there is a ball $\bar A\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}(x')$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{k2}
		\bar A\supset [A],\quad \mu(\bar A)\le \eta \mu([A])\lesssim \mu(A).
	\end{equation}
	Then by definition of $\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}}$ we can write
	\begin{equation}\label{k3}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')\ge \langle f\rangle_{\phi_{\bar A}}.
	\end{equation}
	Using left hand side of \eqref{y4} together with relations \eqref{y2}, \eqref{k1}, \eqref{k2} and \eqref{k3}, we obtain
	\begin{align}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)-\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')&\le \langle f\rangle_{A}-\langle f\rangle_{\phi_{\bar A}}+\delta\\
		&\le \langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar A}}\rangle_{A}+|f_{\phi_{\bar A}}|+\langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar A}}\rangle_{\phi_{\bar A}}-|f_{\phi_{\bar A}}|+\delta\\
		&\lesssim  \langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar A}}\rangle_{\phi_{\bar A}}+\langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar A}}\rangle_{\phi_{\bar A}}+\delta\\
		&\lesssim \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}+\delta.\label{a10}
	\end{align}
	If $\mu(A)\le \mu(B)$, then $x'\in A\subset  [B]$ and 
	\begin{equation}\label{k4}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')\ge \langle f\rangle_{\phi_{\bar B}}
	\end{equation}
	for a ball $\bar B\in \ensuremath{\mathscr B}(x')$ with $\mu(\bar B)\le \eta \mu([B])\lesssim\mu(B)$. Thus, using also \eqref{b1} and \eqref{k1}, we obtain
	\begin{align}\label{a11}
		\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)-\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')&\le \langle f\rangle_{A}-\langle f\rangle_{\phi_{\bar B}}+\delta\\
		&\le \langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\rangle_{A}+\|f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\rangle_{{\phi_{\bar B}}}-\|f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\delta\\
		&\le \langle f-f_{\phi_{B}}\rangle_{A}+\|f_{\phi_B}-f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\|_\ensuremath{\mathbb U}+\langle f-f_{\phi_{\bar B}}\rangle_{{\phi_{\bar B}}}+\delta\\
		&\lesssim \langle g\rangle_{A}+ \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}+\delta\\
		&\le \ensuremath{\mathcal M} g(x)+ \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}+\delta\\
		&\lesssim  (1-\alpha)^{-1/r} \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}+\delta.
	\end{align}
	Since $\delta$ can be arbitrary small, from \eqref{a10} and \eqref{a11} we conclude
	\begin{equation*}
		|\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x)-\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f(x')|\lesssim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r} \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}, \quad x,x'\in E.
	\end{equation*}
	This implies
	\begin{equation}\label{b3}
		{\rm OSC}_E(\ensuremath{\mathcal M}^{\phi,{\mathscr G\,}} f)\lesssim (1-\alpha)^{-1/r} \|\omega\|\langle f\rangle^*_{\#,B}. 
	\end{equation}
	Combining \eqref{b2} and  \eqref{b3}, we deduce \eqref{2}, completing the proof of theorem.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
